triple overlap is the formal initial object. Thus the cocycle condition
for descent is satisfied strictly. Since $\mathfrak L_r$ is a stack,
this descent datum glues to an object
\[
x_\xi\in\mathfrak L_r(a).
\]
By construction, the connected-component class of $x_\xi$ is the original
section $\xi$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Rigidity of representative-rigid lifts over slice sites on which the restricted presheaf is separated]\label{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_a
\]
be a representative-rigid banded groupoid lift with band $\Band$. Let
\[
u:F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\longrightarrow a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\]
be the sheafification unit. Assume throughout this theorem that
\[
F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\]
is separated on the slice in the sense of
\Cref{def:separated-restriction}; equivalently, $F_{p,s}$ is
separated at every object of the slice $\mathcal C_a$, including every
cover member, every pullback overlap, and every refinement occurring below.
Separatedness only at the terminal object $a$ is not sufficient for this
theorem, and no assertion below is made under that weaker hypothesis.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item If $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$, then
\[
u_a:F_{p,s}(a)\to \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)
\]
is surjective.
\item If
\[
H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)=0,
\]
then surjectivity of $u_a$ implies that $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially
surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$.
\end{enumerate}
Under the same vanishing hypothesis the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
\item $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$;
\item $u_a$ is bijective;
\item $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ satisfies the sheaf gluing condition at
the object $a$, that is, every matching family on every cover of $a$ has
an amalgamation in $F_{p,s}(a)$; uniqueness follows from the standing
separatedness on the slice at $a$.
\end{enumerate}
The last equivalence is not a consequence of separatedness at \(a\) alone.
It uses separatedness at all cover members, pullback overlaps, and their
refinements in the slice \(\mathcal C_a\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write
\[
\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r).
\]
By \Cref{prop:stackification-component-unit-naturality}, the morphism of
component sheaves induced by the stackification map
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to \mathfrak L_r
\]
identifies with the sheafification unit
\[
u:F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\to a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\]

Assume first that $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$, and let
\[
\xi\in \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a).
\]
By \Cref{lem:component-section-descent-object}, choose an object
\[
x\in \mathfrak L_r(a)
\]
whose connected-component class is $\xi$. The cited lemma uses the
plus--plus normal form of \Cref{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}:
first one represents $\xi$ by $F_{p,s}$-sections whose overlap equalities
hold after local refinements, then separatedness on the slice upgrades those
refined overlap equalities to a strict matching family, and only then does
effective descent in the stackification produce the terminal object.
Essential surjectivity of
\[
(\iota_r)_a:(\mathfrak P_r)_a\to (\mathfrak L_r)_a
\]
gives an object
\[
y\in \mathfrak P_r(a)
\]
with $\iota_r(y)\cong x$. Let
\[
\sigma:=[y]\in \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(a)\cong F_{p,s}(a).
\]
Since $u_a$ is the map on connected components induced by $\iota_r$, one has
\[
u_a(\sigma)=[\iota_r(y)]=[x]=\xi.
\]
Thus $u_a$ is surjective.

Assume next that $H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)=0$ and that $u_a$ is surjective. Let
\[
x\in \mathfrak L_r(a).
\]
Its connected-component class
\[
[x]\in \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)
\]
lies in the image of $u_a$, so choose
\[
\sigma\in F_{p,s}(a)
\]
with
\[
u_a(\sigma)=[x].
\]
Put
\[
y:=s_a(\sigma)\in \mathfrak P_r(a).
\]
By construction, $y$ represents the class $\sigma$ in $\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(a)$, hence
\[
[\iota_r(y)]=u_a(\sigma)=[x]
\]
in $\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)$. By \Cref{lem:canonical-lift-isom-torsor}, the sheaf
\[
\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L_r}(\iota_r(y),x)
\]
is a $\Band$-torsor, and the vanishing of $H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)$ gives a global section of it. Equivalently, there exists an isomorphism
\[
\iota_r(y)\xrightarrow{\sim} x
\]
in $\mathfrak L_r(a)$. Thus every object of $\mathfrak L_r(a)$ is in the essential image of $(\iota_r)_a$, so $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$.

The standing separatedness on the slice also gives injectivity of the unit at the
terminal object. If
\[
u_a(\sigma)=u_a(\tau)
\qquad
(\sigma,\tau\in F_{p,s}(a)),
\]
then the two global sections define the same element of the sheafification at $a$.
Applying \Cref{lem:sheafification-unit-equality-local} gives a cover
\[
\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
such that
\[
\res_{U_i}^a(\sigma)=\res_{U_i}^a(\tau)
\qquad
(\forall i\in I).
\]
Separatedness on the slice applies in particular at the terminal object
$a$, and therefore forces $\sigma=\tau$. Thus $u_a$ is injective.

Under $H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)=0$, part \textup{(i)} gives
\textup{(a)}$\Rightarrow$\textup{(b)}, and part \textup{(ii)} together
with injectivity gives \textup{(b)}$\Rightarrow$\textup{(a)}. It remains
to identify \textup{(b)} with the terminal sheaf axiom, and we spell this
out because this is the point where separatedness on the slice is
used beyond the terminal object.

Assume \textup{(b)}, and let
\[
\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I},
\qquad
\sigma_i\in F_{p,s}(U_i)
\]
be a strict matching family on a cover of \(a\). Applying \(u\) to the
\(\sigma_i\) gives a matching family in the sheaf
\(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\), hence a unique section
\[
\xi\in \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)
\]
whose restriction to \(U_i\) is \(u_{U_i}(\sigma_i)\). By surjectivity of
\(u_a\), choose \(\sigma\in F_{p,s}(a)\) with \(u_a(\sigma)=\xi\). For
each \(i\),
\[
u_{U_i}\bigl(\res_{U_i}^{a}\sigma\bigr)=u_{U_i}(\sigma_i)
\]
in \((a_{p,s}F_{p,s})(U_i)\). This equality is not an equality in
\(F_{p,s}(U_i)\). By \Cref{lem:sheafification-unit-equality-local},
there is a covering family
\[
\{V_{ik}\to U_i\}_{k}
\]
such that
\[
\res_{V_{ik}}^{a}\sigma=\res_{V_{ik}}^{U_i}\sigma_i
\qquad(\forall k)
\]
in \(F_{p,s}(V_{ik})\). Since \(U_i\) is an object of the slice
\(\mathcal C_a\) and the standing hypothesis is separatedness on the slice,
\(F_{p,s}\) is separated at \(U_i\). The displayed local equalities over
the cover of \(U_i\) therefore force
\[
\res_{U_i}^{a}\sigma=\sigma_i
\]
in \(F_{p,s}(U_i)\). Thus \(\sigma\) is an amalgamation of the original
matching family. Its uniqueness follows from separatedness at \(a\).
Therefore \textup{(c)} holds.

Conversely, assume \textup{(c)} and let
\[
\xi\in \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
\]
By the plus--plus normal form of
\Cref{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}, \(\xi\) is represented
on a cover \(\{U_i\to a\}\) by sections
\(\sigma_i\in F_{p,s}(U_i)\) whose restrictions to each overlap
\[
U_{ij}:=U_i\times_aU_j
\]
agree after a further covering family of \(U_{ij}\). Each \(U_{ij}\) is an
object of the slice \(\mathcal C_a\), so separatedness on the slice and
\Cref{lem:separated-restriction-refined-equality} upgrade these refined
equalities to equalities on the original overlaps. Hence the
\(\sigma_i\) form a strict matching family on the displayed cover. By the
sheaf axiom at \(a\), there is an amalgamation
\(\sigma\in F_{p,s}(a)\). The sections \(u_a(\sigma)\) and \(\xi\) have
the same restrictions to the cover \(\{U_i\to a\}\), so separatedness of
the sheaf \(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\) gives \(u_a(\sigma)=\xi\). Thus \(u_a\) is
surjective, and its injectivity was proved above. Therefore
\textup{(b)} holds.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Matching without amalgamation requires noncanonical lift data]\label{cor:no-rigid-gluing-null}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
assume that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice. If
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r),
\]
then no representative-rigid banded groupoid lift with band $\Band$ can be essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The condition
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\]
means that $r$ is admissible, that
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r),
\]
and that
\[
M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r).
\]
By \Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization},
\[
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\neq\varnothing,

 succeeded in 178ms:
The preceding corollary classifies the componentwise homological image subgroups that can occur on a fixed finite nerve with $H_2\cong \mathbb Z^\beta$. The next theorem replaces that abstract nerve by an explicitly chosen wedge-of-spheres good-cover family and classifies, inside that family and with the stated lift data, the finite abelian groups that occur as two-component label-loss kernels with complementary nonzero image subgroups.

All lift data in this subsection are chosen data: a finite simplicial
model $K_\beta$, the open-star good-cover system, a trivialization of the
constant finite abelian band, and the component cocycles or homomorphisms used in
\Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}. The theorem below is not a canonical
classification over all presentations of the bouquet $\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2$.

\begin{lemma}[Wedge-of-spheres good-cover and finite-abelian interfaces]\label{lem:wedge-sphere-cover-interface}
Let $K$ be a finite simplicial complex and let $\mathcal U^{(m)}$ be the open-star cover of $\operatorname{Sd}^m K$ on $|K|$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item every nonempty finite intersection of members of $\mathcal U^{(m)}$ is contractible, and $N(\mathcal U^{(m)})$ is canonically isomorphic to $\operatorname{Sd}^mK$;
\item the covers $\mathcal U^{(m+1)}$ refine $\mathcal U^{(m)}$, and in the pullback site generated by all such open stars, their nonempty intersections, and a strict initial object for empty intersections, the family $(\mathcal U^{(m)})_{m\ge0}$ is filtered and cofinal;
\item for every constant finite abelian group $G$, the Leray--\v{C}ech comparison identifies
\[
\varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},G)
\cong
H^2(|K|,G),
\]
and the comparison is compatible with the simplicial cohomology of the nerves;
\item for every integer $\beta\ge 1$ and every nonzero finite abelian group $G$, the
group $G$ admits a decomposition
\[
G=G_1\oplus G_2
\]
with $G_1,G_2$ nonzero and $d(G_i)\le\beta$ for $i=1,2$ if and only if $d(G)\le 2\beta$ and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For \textup{(i)}, the open star of a vertex of a barycentric subdivision is contractible, and a nonempty finite intersection of such open stars is the open star of the simplex spanned by the corresponding vertices. This is contractible. The nerve of the open-star cover of a barycentric subdivision is the subdivision itself; this is the standard nerve computation for open stars in a simplicial complex \cite[Secs.~2.1, 4G]{Hatcher2002}.

For \textup{(ii)}, barycentric subdivision gives a simplicial refinement of the previous subdivision, and each open star in $\operatorname{Sd}^{m+1}K$ lies in an open star of $\operatorname{Sd}^{m}K$. The generated site used here includes a strict initial object $\emptyset$ and declares the pullback of two disjoint members to be $\emptyset$; nonempty pullbacks are the corresponding finite intersections. Thus every pullback demanded by the reference-refinement-site axioms is represented in the site, while the \v{C}ech cochains still ignore empty intersections because the constant sheaf has only the zero section on $\emptyset$. The pretopology is generated by these covers, their pullbacks, identity covers, and the empty cover of $\emptyset$, so any covering family in the generated site is refined by a common later barycentric open-star cover. This proves filteredness and cofinality.

For \textup{(iii)}, the contractibility in \textup{(i)} makes each $\mathcal U^{(m)}$ a Leray cover for the genuine constant sheaf $G$ on the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve. Empty pullback objects contribute no cochains. The Leray theorem for \v{C}ech cohomology therefore identifies the \v{C}ech cohomology of these covers with the sheaf cohomology of $|K|$, and the nerve identification in \textup{(i)} identifies the same groups with simplicial cohomology of $\operatorname{Sd}^mK$ \cite[Chap.~III, Sec.~4]{Bredon1997}, \cite[Sec.~4G]{Hatcher2002}. The refinement maps are the usual subdivision maps, hence induce cohomology isomorphisms.

For \textup{(iv)}, fix an integer $\beta\ge1$. The standing nonzero hypothesis removes the trivial group from the classification; here a cyclic $p$-group means a cyclic group of order $p^n$ with $n\ge1$. First suppose such a decomposition exists. Then $G$ is a direct sum of two nonzero groups, so it is decomposable. The finite abelian indecomposable groups are exactly the cyclic groups of prime-power order \cite[Chap.~12]{DummitFoote2004}. Also
\[
d(G)\le d(G_1)+d(G_2)\le 2\beta.
\]
Conversely, assume $d(G)\le2\beta$ and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group. There are two cases: either the minimal number of generators is $m\ge2$, or $m=1$ and the cyclic group has non-prime-power order. If $d(G)=m\ge2$, write an invariant-factor decomposition
\[
G\cong C_{n_1}\oplus\cdots\oplus C_{n_m},
\qquad
n_1\mid n_2\mid\cdots\mid n_m.
\]
With $t=\min\{\beta,m-1\}$, the two sums
\[
G_1=C_{n_1}\oplus\cdots\oplus C_{n_t},
\qquad
G_2=C_{n_{t+1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus C_{n_m}
\]
are nonzero and satisfy $d(G_1)\le\beta$ and $d(G_2)=m-t\le\beta$. If $d(G)=1$, then $G$ is cyclic. Since it is not a cyclic $p$-group, its order has at least two coprime factors $a,b>1$, and the Chinese remainder theorem gives
\[
G\cong \mathbb Z/a\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z/b\mathbb Z,
\]
with both summands nonzero and singly generated. This proves the equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Wedge-of-spheres mixed-component construction transfer]\label{lem:wedge-sphere-mixed-component-transfer}
Fix $\beta\ge 1$ and the open-star good-cover intersection site $\mathcal C_a$ built from
$K_\beta$ and the open-star covers $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$ as in
\Cref{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}. Let $G$ be a finite abelian
group, and let $G_1,G_2\le G$ be nonzero subgroups satisfying
\[
d(G_i)\le \beta\qquad(i=1,2).
\]
Then there exist a state $p$, an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$
inside a model $M$ with $a=[r]_p$, a presheaf of local sections on
$\mathcal C_a$, and a banded groupoid lift with constant band $G$ such
that
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v_1,v_2\},
\qquad
K_{v_i}=G_i\quad(i=1,2).
\]
Both component gerbes are non-neutral. If, moreover,
\[
G_1\cap G_2=0,\qquad G_1+G_2=G,
\]
then
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\cong G,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)=0,
\qquad
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{lem:wedge-sphere-cover-interface}(i)--(iii), the cover
$\mathcal U_\beta^{(0)}$ is a finite good cover whose nerve is
$K_\beta$, all finite intersections are contractible, the constant band
$G$ is trivial on the cover, and the generated filtered family satisfies
\textup{(H4)}. Since $K_\beta\simeq\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2$, we have
\[
H_1\bigl(N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(0)}),\mathbb Z\bigr)=0,
\qquad
H_2\bigl(N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(0)}),\mathbb Z\bigr)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta.
\]
The inequalities $d(G_i)\le\beta$ therefore allow homomorphisms
\[
\phi_i:H_2\bigl(N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(0)}),\mathbb Z\bigr)\to G
\]
with $\operatorname{Im}(\phi_i)=G_i$.

Apply \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction} to the fixed finite nerve,
constant band $A:=G$, component index set $\{1,2\}$, and homomorphisms
$\phi_1,\phi_2$. It constructs the asserted one-state lift data
and gives
\[
K_{v_i}=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_i)=G_i.
\]
The model constructed here is therefore admissible for the same reason as in
\Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}: it is obtained by the layer-expansion
interface of \Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}.
Since $G_i\ne0$, the same theorem makes both component gerbes
non-neutral.

Under the additional direct-sum hypotheses, the intersection of the componentwise homological image subgroups is $0$ and their sum
is all of $G$. The quotient formulas in
\Cref{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient,thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient,thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}
therefore give
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\cong G,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)=0.
\]
Finally, because both component gerbes are non-neutral,
\Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}(iv) gives
$M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Two-component label-loss kernels on chosen good covers of the wedge of $\beta$ $2$-spheres]\label{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}
Fix an integer $\beta\ge 1$, let $K_\beta$ be a finite connected simplicial complex whose geometric realization is homotopy equivalent to
\[
\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta} S^2,
\]
and write
\[
X_\beta:=|K_\beta|.
\]
For each $m\ge 0$, let
\[
\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}
\]
be the open-star cover of the barycentric subdivision $\operatorname{Sd}^m K_\beta$, viewed as a cover of $X_\beta$. Let $\mathcal C_a$ be the pullback site whose objects are $X_\beta$, a strict initial object $\emptyset$, and all nonempty finite intersections of members of the covers $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$; the pullback of two objects over a common target is their nonempty intersection when it is nonempty and is $\emptyset$ otherwise. Covering families are generated by the families $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$, their pullbacks, identity covers, and the empty cover of $\emptyset$.
All typed-expansion construction assertions in this theorem are understood relative to the
typed-expansion convention of
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}.

Then the family
\[
\bigl(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}\bigr)_{m\ge 0}
\]
is a filtered cofinal family satisfying \textup{(H4)} for every constant finite abelian band.

Let $G$ be a finite abelian group, and write $d(G)$ for the minimal number of generators of $G$. Then the following are equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $G\ne0$, $d(G)\le 2\beta$, and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group, where
cyclic $p$-group means cyclic of order $p^n$ with $n\ge1$.
\item In the typed-expansion model class generated by
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}, the one-state
\(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the site \(\mathcal C_a\), a presheaf of local sections on
\(\mathcal C_a\), and a banded groupoid lift
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_a
\]
with trivialized constant band $G$ define a one-state
\(\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3\) model \(M\) with state \(p\) and admissible
parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\), \(a=[r]_p\), using the finite-nerve Leray
comparison supplied by the chosen good covers of the wedge of $\beta$ $2$-spheres, such that
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v_1,v_2\},
\]
both component gerbes $\mathfrak G_{v_1}$ and $\mathfrak G_{v_2}$ are non-neutral, and
\[
K_{v_1}\cap K_{v_2}=0,
\qquad
K_{v_1}+K_{v_2}=G.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Whenever these conditions hold,
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\cong G,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)=0,
\qquad
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We use four explicit interfaces: the good-cover comparison, the
finite-abelian decomposition, the mixed-component construction transfer, and the
component-forgetting exactness consequence.

The good-cover interface is supplied by
\Cref{lem:wedge-sphere-cover-interface}. By clause \textup{(i)},
each $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$ is a finite good cover of $X_\beta$, its
nonempty finite intersections are contractible, and
\[
N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)})\cong \operatorname{Sd}^{m}K_\beta.
\]
Thus the constant band $G$ is trivial on every such cover, and
\[
H^1(U_{i_0\cdots i_n},G)=0
\]
for every nonempty intersection appearing in the \v{C}ech descent
argument. Clause \textup{(ii)} makes
$\bigl(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}\bigr)_{m\ge 0}$ filtered and cofinal in the
generated pretopology of $\mathcal C_a$, and clause \textup{(iii)}
identifies the filtered \v{C}ech system with derived cohomology. Hence
the family satisfies \textup{(H4)} for every constant finite abelian band.

Also, every subdivision of $K_\beta$ is homotopy equivalent to $K_\beta$, hence
\[
H_1\bigl(N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}),\mathbb Z\bigr)=0,
\qquad
H_2\bigl(N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}),\mathbb Z\bigr)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta
\]
for every $m$ \cite[Secs.~2.1, 2.2]{Hatcher2002}. This records exactly
the finite-nerve hypotheses needed by the construction theorems.

Now assume \textup{(i)}. The finite-abelian interface is
\Cref{lem:wedge-sphere-cover-interface}(iv): it gives a direct-sum
decomposition
\[
G=G_1\oplus G_2
\]
with $G_1,G_2$ nonzero and
\[
d(G_1)\le\beta,
\qquad
d(G_2)\le\beta.
\]
The mixed-component construction transfer
\Cref{lem:wedge-sphere-mixed-component-transfer} applied to these two
summands produces a banded groupoid lift with two global component sections, non-neutral
component gerbes, and $K_{v_i}=G_i$. Because the summands form an
internal direct sum, $G_1\cap G_2=0$ and $G_1+G_2=G$, so condition
\textup{(ii)} follows.

Conversely, assume \textup{(ii)} and put
\[
K_i:=K_{v_i}
\qquad
(i=1,2).
\]
For each $i$, choose a finite nerve representative of the component obstruction class $\omega_{v_i}$ on one of the covers $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m_i)}$. Since the good-cover interface gives
\[
H_2\bigl(N(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m_i)}),\mathbb Z\bigr)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta,
\]
the homological image subgroup
\[
K_i=\operatorname{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_{v_i}})
\]
is generated by at most $\beta$ elements. The equalities
\[
K_1\cap K_2=0,
\qquad
K_1+K_2=G
\]
give an internal direct-sum decomposition
\[
G\cong K_1\oplus K_2
\]
with both summands nonzero. Hence $G$ is decomposable, and the finite abelian indecomposable groups are exactly the cyclic $p$-groups \cite[Chap.~12]{DummitFoote2004}. Moreover
\[
d(G)\le d(K_1)+d(K_2)\le 2\beta.
\]
Thus \textup{(i)} holds.

Finally, the exact-sequence consequences are separate from the construction step. The identities
\[
\bigcap_{i=1}^2 K_{v_i}=0,
\qquad
\sum_{i=1}^2 K_{v_i}=G
\]
give
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\cong G,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)=0
\]
by \Cref{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient,thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient,thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}. Since both component gerbes are non-neutral, \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}(iv) gives
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[A two-component sphere example separating the aggregate invariants]\label{ex:two-branch-sphere-separation}
This is the case $\beta=1$ and $G=(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2$ of \Cref{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}.

Let $\mathcal U$ be a finite good cover of $S^2$ with nerve
\[
N:=N(\mathcal U),
\]
and take
\[
A=(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2.
\]
Since
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
\qquad
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong \mathbb Z
\]
\cite[Secs.~2.1, 2.2]{Hatcher2002}, the universal coefficient theorem gives
\[
H^2(N,A)\cong \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)\cong A.
\]
Choose simplicial cocycles
\[
\alpha_1,\alpha_2\in Z^2(N,A)
\]
whose cohomology classes evaluate on the fundamental class $[S^2]$ as the basis vectors $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$. Define the presheaf of local sections
\[
F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}:\mathcal C_a^{op}\to\mathsf{Set}
\]
by
\[
F_{p,s}(U)=\{v_1,v_2\}
\]
for every nonempty proper finite intersection $U$ of members of $\mathcal U$, by
\[
F_{p,s}(a)=\varnothing,
\]
and by the identity map on the labels $v_1,v_2$ under restriction. This presheaf
of local sections and the prestack data below are used in the typed-expansion
convention of \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}. Then
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r),
\qquad
M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r),
\]
and the sheafification has exactly two global component sections, represented by the constant families $v_1$ and $v_2$ on the cover. For $m=1,2$, choose lift objects $x_i^{(m)}$ over the members of $\mathcal U$ with automorphism group $A$ and transition maps whose triple-overlap defect is $\alpha_m$. This is the two-label instance of the cocycle-to-prestack construction isolated in \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}; equivalently, it is the standard local-trivialization construction for gerbes \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Sec.~5]{Breen2006}, \cite{Murray1996}. It yields a prestack presentation $\mathfrak P^{(m)}$ with
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P^{(m)})
\]
equal to the singleton subpresheaf of $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ generated by $v_m$, and its stackification
\[
\mathfrak G_{v_m}:=a(\mathfrak P^{(m)})
\]
is the corresponding non-neutral $A$-banded component gerbe.

Form the disjoint union prestack
\[
\mathfrak P_r:=\mathfrak P^{(1)}\sqcup \mathfrak P^{(2)}
\]
and let
\[
\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r).
\]
Its presheaf of isomorphism classes is the disjoint union of the two singleton subpresheaves supplied by $\mathfrak P^{(1)}$ and $\mathfrak P^{(2)}$, hence
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\]
Therefore
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v_1,v_2\},
\]
while both component gerbes are non-neutral. Thus the two global component sections are recovered from explicit presheaf sections plus chosen lift objects, rather than inserted by taking gerbes as primitive input.

If $[S^2]$ denotes the fundamental class in $H_2(N,\mathbb Z)$, then
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_1}([S^2])=(1,0),
\qquad
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_2}([S^2])=(0,1).
\]
Hence
\[
K_{v_1}=\langle(1,0)\rangle,
\qquad
K_{v_2}=\langle(0,1)\rangle,
\qquad
Q_{v_1}\cong Q_{v_2}\cong \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z.
\]
Therefore
\[

 succeeded in 194ms:
The functoriality invoked here is the usual naturality of Giraud's classification with respect to a morphism of bands. Equivalently, the assignment
\[
(\mathfrak G,\Band)\longmapsto [\mathfrak G]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\Band)
\]
is functorial for banded equivalences: an equivalence of gerbes whose automorphism sheaves are identified by $\theta$ transports a local descent-defect cocycle to its pointwise image under $\theta$, and this construction is independent of all choices after passage to cohomology.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Canonical component-section obstruction map under banded stack lifts]\label{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let
\[
(\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r)
\]
be a $\Band$-banded stack lift of the component sheaf in the sense of \Cref{def:banded-stack-lift}. Then the assignment
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}:\Vis_{p,s}(r)\longrightarrow H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band),
\qquad
v\longmapsto [\mathfrak E_r[v]]
\]
is canonically defined, where $[\mathfrak E_r[v]]$ denotes the Giraud class of the $v$-component substack. More precisely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every sheafified global section $v$, the full substack $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is a $\Band$-banded gerbe on $\mathcal C_a$;
\item for every sheafified global section $v$,
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak E_r[v]\ \text{is neutral}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak E_r[v](a)\neq\varnothing;
\]
\item if
\[
(\mathfrak E'_r,\Band',\epsilon'_r)
\]
is another banded stack lift of the component sheaf and
\[
\Phi:\mathfrak E_r\to \mathfrak E'_r
\]
is an equivalence of stacks whose induced map on automorphism sheaves identifies the bands through an isomorphism
\[
\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'
\]
and whose map on connected components satisfies
\[
\epsilon'_r\circ \pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r,
\]
then for every sheafified global section $v$ the functor $\Phi$ restricts to an equivalence
\[
\mathfrak E_r[v]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak E'_r[v]
\]
and
\[
\theta_*\bigl(\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)\bigr)=\delta_{\mathfrak E'_r}(v)
\quad\text{in}\quad
H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band');
\]
\item if $r$ admits a gluing-sensitive banded lift datum and
\[
\mathfrak E_r=\mathfrak L_r=a(\mathfrak P_r)
\]
is its stackification, then
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=\omega_v
\]
for every global component section $v$ for which \textup{(H4)} holds.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a sheafified global section
\[
v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
\]
Since $\mathfrak E_r$ is a stack in groupoids and $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is a full substack, $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is again a stack in groupoids.

Let $U$ be an object of $\mathcal C_a$. The section
\[
v|_U\in \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(U)\cong \pi_0(\mathfrak E_r)(U)
\]
is, by definition of the sheaf of connected components, locally represented by objects of $\mathfrak E_r$. Hence there exists a cover
\[
\{U_i\to U\}
\]
and objects
\[
x_i\in \mathfrak E_r(U_i)
\]
with
\[
\epsilon_{r,U_i}([x_i])=v|_{U_i}.
\]
Therefore
\[
(\mathfrak E_r[v])(U_i)\neq\varnothing
\]
for every $i$, so $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is locally nonempty.

Now let
\[
x,y\in \mathfrak E_r[v](U).
\]
Then
\[
\epsilon_{r,U}([x])=\epsilon_{r,U}([y])=v|_U.
\]
Because $\epsilon_{r,U}$ is an isomorphism, the connected-component classes $[x]$ and $[y]$ in $\pi_0(\mathfrak E_r)(U)$ coincide. By the definition of $\pi_0(\mathfrak E_r)$, there exists a cover
\[
\{U_i\to U\}
\]
such that
\[
x|_{U_i}\cong y|_{U_i}
\qquad
(\forall i).
\]
Thus any two objects of $\mathfrak E_r[v](U)$ are locally isomorphic. The full substack $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is therefore a gerbe \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}. Since it is full inside the $\Band$-banded stack $\mathfrak E_r$, the automorphism sheaf of every object in $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is still identified with $\Band$.

The preceding sentence is the band-preservation lemma used in this
argument: if \(P\) is \(\Band\)-banded and \(L=a(P)\), every object of \(L\)
is locally in the essential image of \(P\), and the automorphism sheaf of
that local representative is \(\Band\). Because automorphism presheaves are
sheafified in the stackification and the band identifications are compatible
with restriction, these local identifications descend and glue on overlaps.
Consequently every object of \(L\), and every object of a full component
substack, has automorphism sheaf locally identified with \(\Band\) in a way
compatible with descent. This proves \textup{(i)}.

Clause \textup{(i)} identifies $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ as a $\Band$-banded gerbe, so its Giraud class
\[
[\mathfrak E_r[v]]\in H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
\]
is defined canonically \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}. By the classification of banded gerbes, this class vanishes if and only if the gerbe is neutral. Since $a$ is the terminal object of the slice site $\mathcal C_a$, neutrality is equivalent to the existence of an object over $a$, namely
\[
\mathfrak E_r[v](a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
This proves \textup{(ii)}.

Assume now that $\Phi$ is an equivalence as in \textup{(iii)}. The compatibility condition
\[
\epsilon'_r\circ\pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r
\]
first fixes the component on which the equivalence is allowed to act: an object whose component is $v|_U$ is sent to an object whose component is again $v|_U$. Hence \Cref{lem:extension-equivalence-component-transport} gives a restricted equivalence
\[
\Phi_v:\mathfrak E_r[v]\to \mathfrak E'_r[v]
\]
of the two $v$-component gerbes. This equivalence is not merely an abstract equivalence of stacks: on every lift object $x$ the induced map
\[
\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak E_r[v]}(x)\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak E'_r[v]}(\Phi_v x)
\]
is the band map $\theta:\Band\to\Band'$. Therefore the local-trivialization cocycle defining the Giraud class of $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is transported termwise by $\theta$ to the cocycle defining the class of $\mathfrak E'_r[v]$. By the band-functorial classification recalled in \Cref{lem:giraud-class-transport},
\[
\theta_*\bigl([\mathfrak E_r[v]]\bigr)=[\mathfrak E'_r[v]]
\]
in $H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band')$. This is exactly the displayed identity in \textup{(iii)}.

For \textup{(iv)}, let $\mathfrak E_r=\mathfrak L_r=a(\mathfrak P_r)$. By \Cref{prop:stackification-components}, the stackification carries a canonical identification
\[
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a},
\]
so $\mathfrak L_r$ is a banded stack lift of the component sheaf. The $v$-component substack defined above is then exactly the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ of \Cref{def:component-gerbes}. On every cover
\[
\mathcal U\in \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a),
\]
\Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction} constructs the \v{C}ech class of this gerbe. The filtered colimit of those \v{C}ech classes is, by the standard classification of banded gerbes, the Giraud class of $\mathfrak G_v$, and hypothesis \textup{(H4)} identifies that colimit with the derived cohomology group
\[
H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band).
\]
By \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}, the image of that same filtered system is precisely $\omega_v$. Therefore
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=[\mathfrak G_v]=\omega_v.
\]
\end{proof}
\]
By \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}, the image of that same filtered system is precisely $\omega_v$. Therefore
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=[\mathfrak G_v]=\omega_v.
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Supporting component gerbes of global component sections]\label{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition}
In the pullback-based setting of \Cref{def:component-gerbes}, and using only the banded groupoid lift and component data fixed there, every global component section $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$ determines a full substack $\mathfrak G_v\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ that is a banded $\Band$-gerbe over $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$. In particular, neither essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre over $a$ nor the derived comparison hypothesis \textup{(H4)} is used here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{prop:stackification-components}, the stackification
\[
\mathfrak E_r:=\mathfrak L_r
\]
is a $\Band$-banded stack lift of the component sheaf of
$a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$. The global component section $v$ is a section of
\[
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)\cong (a_{p,s}F_{p,s})(a),
\]
and the full substack
\[
\mathfrak G_v(U)=
\{x\in\mathfrak L_r(U)\mid [x]\ \text{restricts to}\ v|_U\}
\]
is therefore exactly the $v$-component substack
$\mathfrak E_r[v]$ of \Cref{def:banded-stack-lift}.

We recall why this is a gerbe. Because $\mathfrak L_r$ is a stack in
groupoids, every descent datum for objects whose component lies over
$v$ glues to an object whose component still lies over $v$; hence
$\mathfrak G_v$ is a full substack. Local nonemptiness follows from the
definition of a global component section as a section of the sheaf of connected
components: after passing to a cover of any object $U$, the section
$v|_U$ is represented by objects of $\mathfrak L_r$. Local connectedness
is the local form of equality in the component sheaf: if two objects of
$\mathfrak G_v(U)$ have component $v|_U$, then they become isomorphic
after a cover of $U$. Fullness is built into the definition of
$\mathfrak G_v$, so no morphisms between objects in the component are
discarded. Finally, for every object $x$ of $\mathfrak G_v(U)$, the
automorphism sheaf of $x$ inside $\mathfrak G_v$ is the same as its
automorphism sheaf inside $\mathfrak L_r$, and this sheaf is identified
with $\Band|_{\mathcal C_a/U}$ by the banding of the groupoid lift
prestack and its stackification. Thus $\mathfrak G_v$ is a
$\Band$-banded gerbe.

This argument uses only the banded groupoid lift, its stackification, and
the component identification. Essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre concerns
whether terminal stack objects come from terminal prestack objects, and
\textup{(H4)} concerns promotion of cover-level \v{C}ech classes to
derived cohomology; neither condition enters the gerbe construction
itself.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech obstruction of a component gerbe on an acyclic cover]\label{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction}
Assume that $\mathfrak G_v$ is a banded component gerbe with band $\Band$ in the sense of \Cref{def:component-gerbes}. Using only this gerbe and a single acyclic cover of $a$, let
\[
\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in \Cov_{p,s}(a)
\]
be any cover whose \v{C}ech nerve exists in $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$, and write
\[
U_{i_0\cdots i_n}
\]
for the corresponding simplices. Assume that the component gerbe is locally nonempty on the cover, so that objects
\[
x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)
\]
exist for all $i$, and assume
\[
H^1(U_{i_0\cdots i_n},\Band)=0
\]
for every nonempty intersection used below, including all members, pairwise overlaps, triple overlaps, and quadruple overlaps appearing in the descent calculation.
Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every pair $(i,j)$ there exists an isomorphism
\[
\theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}};
\]
\item on each triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the element
\[
c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
\in \Band(U_{ijk})
\]
defines a \v{C}ech $2$-cocycle $c=(c_{ijk})\in \check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$;
\item the class $[c]\in \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$ depends only on the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ and the chosen cover: changing the local lift objects or the comparison isomorphisms changes $c$ by a \v{C}ech coboundary, and refinements of acyclic covers pull this class back functorially;
\item the class $[c]$ vanishes if and only if the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral over $a$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By local nonemptiness, fix objects $x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)$. For every pair $(i,j)$, the sheaf
\[
\mathrm{Isom}(x_i|_{U_{ij}},x_j|_{U_{ij}})
\]
is a $\Band|_{U_{ij}}$-torsor. The vanishing
\[
H^1(U_{ij},\Band)=0
\]
therefore trivializes this torsor \cite[Chap.~III]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}, \cite[Sec.~11]{StacksProject}, so we may choose a section
\[
\theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}}.
\]
This proves \textup{(i)}.

On a triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the composite
\[
c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
\]
is an automorphism of $x_i|_{U_{ijk}}$ and hence an element of $\Band(U_{ijk})$. On a quadruple overlap $U_{ijkl}$ one computes
\[
c_{jkl}\,c_{ijl}
=
\theta_{jl}^{-1}\theta_{kl}\theta_{jk}\,\theta_{il}^{-1}\theta_{jl}\theta_{ij}
=
\theta_{il}^{-1}\theta_{kl}\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{ik}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}
=
c_{ikl}\,c_{ijk},
\]
where all restrictions to $U_{ijkl}$ are suppressed from the notation. This is exactly the \v{C}ech cocycle identity $\delta c=0$, so $c=(c_{ijk})$ lies in $\check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$. This proves \textup{(ii)}.

For \textup{(iii)}, first keep the objects $x_i$ fixed and replace $\theta_{ij}$ by another choice $\theta'_{ij}$. Since $\mathrm{Isom}(x_i|_{U_{ij}},x_j|_{U_{ij}})$ is a $\Band|_{U_{ij}}$-torsor, there exist unique sections $h_{ij}\in \Band(U_{ij})$ with
\[
\theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
\]
A direct calculation on triple overlaps gives
\[
c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1},
\]
which is the multiplicative form of the statement that $c'$ and $c$ differ by the coboundary $\delta h$.

Next replace the objects $x_i$ by another family $x_i'\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)$. Since $x_i$ and $x_i'$ lie in the same gerbe over $U_i$, the sheaf $\mathrm{Isom}(x_i,x_i')$ is a $\Band|_{U_i}$-torsor. The vanishing
\[
H^1(U_i,\Band)=0
\]
trivializes it, so choose isomorphisms
\[
\psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim} x_i'.
\]
Define
\[
\theta_{ij}':=\psi_j|_{U_{ij}}\circ \theta_{ij}\circ \psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ij}}.
\]
Then the associated cocycle satisfies
\[
c'_{ijk}
=
\psi_i|_{U_{ijk}}\,c_{ijk}\,\psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}
=
c_{ijk},
\]
under the band identification $\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i)\cong \Band|_{U_i}$. Thus changing local lift objects changes the cocycle only by the preliminary freedom in the choice of the $\psi_i$, hence only by a coboundary. If
\[
\lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U
\]
is a refinement by another cover $\mathcal V\in\Cov_{p,s}(a)$ whose \v{C}ech nerve exists and whose relevant intersections satisfy the same $H^1$-vanishing hypothesis, then the same construction on $\mathcal V$ uses the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms, so the resulting cocycle is exactly $\lambda^*(c)$. Therefore the induced cohomology class is refinement compatible.

For \textup{(iv)}, suppose first that $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral and choose a global object $x\in \mathfrak G_v(a)$. For each $i$, the sheaf $\mathrm{Isom}(x|_{U_i},x_i)$ is a $\Band|_{U_i}$-torsor and therefore trivial because $H^1(U_i,\Band)=0$. Choose
\[
\psi_i:x|_{U_i}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_i.
\]
If we set
\[
\theta_{ij}:=\psi_j|_{U_{ij}}\circ \psi_i^{-1}|_{U_{ij}},
\]
then the resulting cocycle is identically zero, so $[c]=0$.

Conversely, assume $[c]=0$. Then there is a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with
\[
c=\delta h.
\]
Replace $\theta_{ij}$ by
\[
\theta_{ij}':=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
\]
The defining equation $c=\delta h$ is exactly the statement that the $\theta_{ij}'$ satisfy the strict cocycle condition on triple overlaps. Hence $(x_i,\theta_{ij}')$ is a descent datum for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, and since $\mathfrak G_v$ is a stack in groupoids, that descent datum is effective. The resulting glued object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$ is a global object, so the gerbe is neutral.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\v{C}ech calculus for component gerbes]\label{lem:cech-branch-gerbe-calculus}
In the setting of \Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction}, the following four operations have the indicated effects on the cocycle
\[
c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}.
\]
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item If the local lift objects $x_i$ are fixed and $\theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$ for a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with values in $\Band$, then the new cocycle is
\[
c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1};
\]
in additive notation, $c'-c=\delta h$.
\item If local lift objects $x_i$ are replaced by $x_i'$ and $\psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim}x_i'$ are chosen, then
\[
\theta'_{ij}:=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
\]
produces the same cocycle under the band identifications. Changing the $\psi_i$ modifies the result only by a coboundary of the kind in \textup{(i)}.
\item If $\lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U$ is a refinement satisfying the same acyclicity hypotheses, the cocycle constructed from the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms on $\mathcal V$ is $\lambda^*c$.
\item The class $[c]$ is zero if and only if the descent datum may be adjusted so that the comparison isomorphisms satisfy the strict cocycle condition; by effective descent for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, this is equivalent to a global object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For \textup{(i)}, the Isom sheaf on $U_{ij}$ is a $\Band$-torsor, so the ratio between $\theta_{ij}$ and $\theta'_{ij}$ is the unique element $h_{ij}^{-1}\in\Band(U_{ij})$ with
\[
\theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}.
\]
Substituting these three identities in
\[
c'_{ijk}=(\theta'_{ik})^{-1}\theta'_{jk}\theta'_{ij}
\]
and using that the band is abelian gives
\[
c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1}.
\]
This is precisely the multiplicative Cech coboundary formula.

For \textup{(ii)}, substituting
\[
\theta'_{ij}=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
\]
into the triple product gives
\[
(\theta'_{ik})^{-1}\theta'_{jk}\theta'_{ij}
=
\psi_i\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}.
\]
Under the band identification
\[
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i)\cong\Band|_{U_i}\cong
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(x_i'),
\]
conjugation by $\psi_i$ is the identity on $\Band$. If the isomorphisms $\psi_i$ are changed, the new choices differ from the old ones by a $0$-cochain in the automorphism sheaves, and the resulting comparison isomorphisms differ from the displayed $\theta'_{ij}$ by the coboundary described in \textup{(i)}.

For \textup{(iii)}, a refinement map $\lambda$ sends each member of $\mathcal V$ to one member of $\mathcal U$. Pulling back the objects $x_i$ and maps $\theta_{ij}$ along these assignments gives on each triple overlap of $\mathcal V$ exactly the restriction of $c_{\lambda(i)\lambda(j)\lambda(k)}$. This is the usual pullback cocycle $\lambda^*c$.

For \textup{(iv)}, if $[c]=0$, choose $h$ with $c=\delta h$ and replace $\theta_{ij}$ by $\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$. By \textup{(i)} the new cocycle is zero, which says exactly that the modified $\theta_{ij}$ satisfy the strict descent condition on triple overlaps. Since $\mathfrak G_v$ is a stack, this descent datum is effective and glues to an object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$. Conversely, a global object $x\in\mathfrak G_v(a)$ and local isomorphisms $x|_{U_i}\cong x_i$ produce comparison maps whose triple defect is zero; \textup{(ii)} and \textup{(i)} then show that the original cocycle class is zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Derived component classes detect neutrality]\label{prop:derived-branch-classification}
Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H4)}. For every global component section $v$,
\[
\omega_v=0\ \text{in}\ H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}, the derived class $\omega_v$ is the image of the refinement-compatible \v{C}ech classes supplied by \Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction}. The comparison isomorphism in \textup{(H4)} identifies this class with the standard Giraud class of the banded gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$. The vanishing of that derived class is equivalent to neutrality by the classification of banded gerbes by $H^2$ \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Matching families versus global component sections]\label{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches}
Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admissible at $p$, admits a banded groupoid lift in the sense of \Cref{def:banded-groupoid-lift}, and that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice. Then global component sections detect the existence of matching families, without any terminal-fibre essential-surjectivity or derived-comparison hypothesis:
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{thm:visible-value-components},
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
\]
The implication from strict existence of a matching family to
nonemptiness of this sheafification is
\Cref{prop:compsec-to-sheafification}. Conversely, a global component section gives
an element of \((a_{p,s}F_{p,s})(a)\) by the same displayed
identification. Separatedness on the slice is exactly the additional
condition needed to choose a matching-family representative of that sheafified section: applying
\Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization} to
\(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) produces a cover of \(a\) and a strict
matching family of \(F_{p,s}\)-sections on that cover. By
\Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii), this is
\[
M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Terminal-fibre nonemptiness and neutrality]\label{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}
Let $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$. Then
\[
\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
\]
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_terminal_fibre_neutrality}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By definition of the gerbe notion used in this paper, neutrality means the existence of a global object over $a$. Therefore it is exactly the same as $\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Global sections versus neutral component gerbes]\label{prop:global-sections-vs-neutral-components}
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_global_sections_vs_neutral_components}
Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admissible at $p$, that it admits a banded groupoid lift in the sense of \Cref{def:banded-groupoid-lift}, and that this prestack is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$ in the sense of \Cref{def:global-conservativity}. Then a global lift object exists exactly when some global component section has a global object:
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\exists v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\ \text{such that}\ \mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
Equivalently, by \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}, a global lift object exists exactly when at least one global component section gerbe is neutral.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
From \Cref{prop:no-new-global-objects}, $M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)$ iff $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$.

If $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$, choose $x\in \mathfrak L_r(a)$ and let
\[
v=[x]\in \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)\cong \Vis_{p,s}(r).
\]
Then $x\in \mathfrak G_v(a)$, so $\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing$.

Conversely, if $\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing$ for some global component section $v$, then $\mathfrak G_v(a)\subseteq \mathfrak L_r(a)$, so $\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Component-gerbe criterion for matching without amalgamation]\label{thm:gerbe-null-semantics}
Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H3)} for an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at $p$, and assume in addition that \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is separated on the slice. Then matching without amalgamation is equivalent to the existence of at least one global component section and the non-neutrality of every component gerbe:
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
\ \text{and}\
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is non-neutral for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r).
\]
If, in addition, \textup{(H4)} holds, then this is equivalently
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
\ \text{and}\
\omega_v\neq 0\ \text{in }H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
\text{ for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r).
\]
The companion theorem \cite[Theorem~5.20]{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} is the meet-semilattice analogue of this pullback-site formulation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Because $r$ is admissible, the definition of matching without amalgamation gives
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)\ \text{and}\ M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r).
\]
By \Cref{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches}, the compatible-local clause is equivalent to
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing.
\]
By \Cref{prop:global-sections-vs-neutral-components}, using essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre over $a$ from \textup{(H3)}, the global-section clause is equivalent to the existence of a global component section $v$ with
\[
\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
By \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}, this is equivalent to the existence of a neutral component gerbe. Therefore
\[
M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is non-neutral for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r),
\]
provided the compatible-local clause has supplied at least one global component section. Combining these two equivalences proves the first displayed characterization.

Assume now also \textup{(H4)}. For each global component section $v$, \Cref{prop:derived-branch-classification} gives
\[
\omega_v=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
\]
Thus non-neutrality of every component gerbe is equivalent to nonvanishing of every derived component class $\omega_v$, and the second displayed characterization follows.
\end{proof}
