\subsection{The exact interface with empirical models}

Abramsky--Brandenburger empirical models already have two categorical
reformulations close to the question considered here. Car\`u identifies the
degree-one cohomology used by Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa with torsors
trivialized by the measurement cover \cite[Prop.~6.3]{Caru2017}. More recently,
Barbosa, Kharoof, and Okay construct fully faithful functors from measurement
scenarios to bundle and simplicial-bundle scenarios, together with natural
isomorphisms on their empirical-model functors
\cite{BarbosaKharoofOkay2024}. Neither construction produces a banded gerbe
or a banded stack lift in the sense of
\Cref{def:banded-stack-lift}. The following comparison locates the
gap precisely.

Let a \emph{finite measurement scenario} be a finite set \(X\), finite
nonempty outcome sets \(O_x\) for \(x\in X\), and a finite antichain
\(\mathcal M\) of subsets of \(X\) whose union is \(X\). Write
\(\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}\) for the finite context category consisting of
the subcontexts of members of \(\mathcal M\), with inclusions as arrows, and
equip it with the coverage induced by the maximal contexts. We also use its
terminal augmentation, obtained by adjoining \(X\) and declaring
\(\mathcal M\) to cover \(X\). A finite empirical model
\[
e=(e_C)_{C\in\mathcal M}
\]
is a compatible family of probability distributions on
\(\prod_{x\in C}O_x\). Its support presheaf is
\[
S_e(U)=\operatorname{supp}(e_U)
\qquad(U\in\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}),
\]
where compatibility makes the marginal \(e_U\) independent of the maximal
context containing \(U\) \cite{AbramskyBrandenburger2011}. At the adjoined
terminal object we take the right Kan extension
\[
\widehat S_e(X):=
\varprojlim_{U\in\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}}S_e(U).
\]
Thus an element of \(\widehat S_e(X)\) is, by definition, a compatible family
of supported local assignments. Below \(aS_e\) denotes the sheafification of
this terminally augmented presheaf.

A \emph{context refinement} used below is a refinement
\(\rho:\mathcal M'\to\mathcal M\) on the same measurements and outcomes,
with \(C'\subseteq\rho(C')\), for which marginalization defines the refined
model \(\rho^*e\). The inclusion of downward-closed context categories induces
a functor
\[
u_\rho:\mathcal D_{\mathcal M'}\hookrightarrow
\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}
\]
which is required to be continuous for the two generated cover topologies and
to extend to their terminal augmentations. We also require the natural
base-change isomorphism
\[
S_{\rho^*e}\cong u_\rho^*S_e.
\]
This includes ordinary iterated refinements which are covering refinements
for the generated topology. No
claim is made here for resource-theoretic simulations which change the
measurement or outcome sets.

\begin{proposition}[Abramsky--Brandenburger support interface]\label{prop:ab-compatibility-translation}
For every finite empirical model \(e\), the following sets are naturally in
bijection:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item compatible families \((s_C)_{C\in\mathcal M}\) with
      \(s_C\in S_e(C)\);
\item global assignments \(s\in\prod_{x\in X}O_x\) such that
      \(s|_C\in S_e(C)\) for every \(C\in\mathcal M\);
\item global sections of the support presheaf in the standard
      Abramsky--Brandenburger sense;
\item elements of \(\widehat S_e(X)\), equivalently elements of
      \((aS_e)(X)\) on the terminally augmented context site.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, if both \(\CompSec_s(r)\) and \(\Sec_s(r)\) are interpreted
from this same support presheaf on the standard measurement cover, then
\[
M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash\Sec_s(r).
\]
In particular, strong contextuality is absence of a compatible family at the
set-valued level; it is not the matching-without-amalgamation case
\(\CompSec_s(r)\land\neg\Sec_s(r)\) of this paper.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The event presheaf
\(\mathcal E(U)=\prod_{x\in U}O_x\) is a sheaf. Hence a compatible family
\((s_C)\) glues uniquely to a global assignment \(s\), and its restrictions
remain in the prescribed supports. Conversely, restriction of such an
assignment gives a compatible supported family. This proves the first three
identifications. The fourth is the defining limit formula for
\(\widehat S_e(X)\); the plus construction for the cover
\(\mathcal M\to X\) gives the same set in the sheafification. The displayed
equivalence follows immediately.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Support versus probabilistic contextuality]\label{rem:support-vs-probabilistic-contextuality}
The proposition concerns the possibilistic support of \(e\). Probabilistic
noncontextuality asks instead for a distribution on
\(\prod_{x\in X}O_x\) whose marginals are the given distributions \(e_C\).
That is a fibre-extension problem for the distribution presheaf, and is not
determined by \(S_e\): empirical models with the same support can have
different probabilistic noncontextuality behaviour.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Canonical split stack and the degree obstruction]\label{thm:empirical-model-stack-interface}
Let \(e\) be a finite empirical model on the context site above.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item There is a canonical banded stack lift with zero band,
\[
\mathfrak E_e^0:=\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e),
\qquad
\pi_0(\mathfrak E_e^0)\cong aS_e.
\]
Here \(\operatorname{Disc}\) regards a sheaf of sets as a stack of discrete
groupoids.
\item More generally, after an abelian sheaf \(\Band\) has been supplied,
there is a canonical split \(\Band\)-banded stack lift
\[
\mathfrak E_e^{\mathrm{spl}}:=
\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e)\times\mathsf B\Band.
\]
Every component gerbe of this lift is neutral, and therefore every
Giraud class supplied by
\Cref{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension} is zero.
\item The assignments in \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} are pseudonatural
under context refinements: there are coherent banded equivalences
\[
u_\rho^{-1}\mathfrak E_e^0\simeq
\mathfrak E_{\rho^*e}^0,
\qquad
u_\rho^{-1}\mathfrak E_e^{\mathrm{spl}}\simeq
\mathfrak E_{\rho^*e}^{\mathrm{spl}}
\]
in the second formula whenever the supplied bands are identified under
pullback.
\item The AB and AMB constructions do not produce the non-split degree-two
data required by this paper. More precisely, fix
\(C_0\in\mathcal M\) and \(s\in S_e(C_0)\).
Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa form the free-abelian presheaf
\(\mathcal F_e=\mathbb Z[S_e]\), its relative kernel
\(\mathcal F_{e,\overline C_0}\), and a class
\[
\gamma_e(s)\in
\check H^1(\mathcal M,\mathcal F_{e,\overline C_0}).
\]
This is relative \v{C}ech cohomology of a presheaf, and the class is the
isomorphism class of an
\(\mathcal F_{e,\overline C_0}\)-torsor trivialized by
\(\mathcal M\). It is not a derived \(H^2\) Giraud class for a sheaf band
\cite{AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012,Caru2017}.
\item There is no comparison which recovers these AMB classes, for all finite
empirical models, from the component-section obstruction map of any banded stack lift
whose component sheaf is \(aS_e\). Indeed, if \(e\) is strongly contextual,
then
\[
(aS_e)(X)=\varnothing,
\]
so the component-section obstruction map
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_e}:(aS_e)(X)\longrightarrow
H^2(\mathcal D_{\mathcal M},\Band)
\]
has empty domain, whereas AMB classes are defined for the local sections
\(s\in S_e(C_0)\) and can be nonzero, as in their GHZ and
Kochen--Specker examples.
\end{enumerate}
In \textup{(i)}, \emph{canonical} means that the construction is the
composite of the universal sheafification functor and the discrete-stack
functor, with no choices, and that the displayed refinement equivalences
satisfy the usual pseudofunctor coherence. It does not mean that all
banded stack lifts of \(S_e\) are equivalent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The discrete groupoid construction preserves descent, so
\(\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e)\) is a stack and its connected-component sheaf is
exactly \(aS_e\). Its automorphism sheaves are zero, proving \textup{(i)}.
The classifying stack \(\mathsf B\Band\) has one local component and
automorphism sheaf \(\Band\). Taking its product with the discrete stack
therefore proves the band and component assertions in \textup{(ii)}. The
global object represented by the identity \(\Band\)-torsor neutralizes each
component gerbe, so its Giraud class vanishes.

Inverse image for the morphism of context sites commutes, up to its canonical
isomorphism, with associated sheaves, discrete stacks, products, and
classifying stacks. Together with
\(S_{\rho^*e}\cong u_\rho^*S_e\), this gives the equivalences and their
coherence in \textup{(iii)}.

The construction of \(\gamma_e(s)\) and its degree are those of
\cite[Sec.~4]{AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012}. The torsor
classification is \cite[Prop.~6.3]{Caru2017}, proving \textup{(iv)}. Finally,
\Cref{prop:ab-compatibility-translation} identifies strong contextuality with
emptiness of \((aS_e)(X)\). The component-section obstruction map of
\Cref{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension} is indexed by precisely
that set, while \(\gamma_e(s)\) is indexed by a local section. This proves
the asserted failure in \textup{(v)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exact limit of transfer to empirical models]\label{thm:unique-branch-contextuality-comparison}
For finite Abramsky--Brandenburger empirical models, the set-valued
local-to-global statements of \Cref{prop:ab-compatibility-translation}
transfer functorially. The later banded and homological theorems transfer
only after a band and a non-split banded stack lift have been
supplied as additional data. In particular:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the canonical split lift gives only zero Giraud classes and zero
homological image subgroups;
\item the characterization
\[
d(G)\le 2\beta
\quad\text{and}\quad
G\text{ is not a cyclic }p\text{-group}
\]
remains a construction theorem for the chosen wedge-of-spheres good-cover, band, and
two-component cocycle data of
\Cref{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}; it is not a characterization of
groups canonically attached to empirical models;
\item without an additional degree-shifting construction, the Giraud
\(H^2\)-class neither recovers nor refines the AMB relative
\(\check H^1\)-obstruction.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The first and third assertions are
\Cref{thm:empirical-model-stack-interface}(ii),(iv)--(v). The wedge-of-spheres theorem
starts from a prescribed finite nerve, a constant finite abelian band, and
chosen componentwise homomorphisms
\(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A\). None of those data is selected by a bare empirical
model, so its group classification cannot be transported through the split
construction. This proves the second assertion.
\end{proof}

 succeeded in 175ms:
\begin{theorem}[Typed-expansion construction with neutral and non-neutral component gerbes on a finite intersection site]\label{thm:mixed-component-construction}
Let
\[
\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
be a finite good-cover presentation in a pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$ whose nonempty \v{C}ech nerve
\[
N:=N(\mathcal U)
\]
is connected. Equivalently, the geometric realization of the cover nerve is a
paracompact space homotopy equivalent to $|N|$ and all nonempty finite
intersections of members of $\mathcal U$ are acyclic for constant
coefficients. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}; it contains the strict
initial object $\emptyset$ for empty intersections, and the \v{C}ech
cochain complex below omits those pullback objects only because
$A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0$. Let $A$ be a finite abelian group, and let
$A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine constant sheaf on the finite
intersection site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ whose value on each nonempty
basic intersection is identified with $A$. View $A_{\mathcal U}$ as the
automorphism band of the banded groupoid lift constructed below, and
assume
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Use the Leray comparison isomorphism of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv). The typed-expansion
construction below is relative to the deliberately broad
typed-expansion semantics of
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}. Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, and let
\[
\phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
be a homomorphism for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$. Define
\[
\Lambda_0:=\{\lambda\in\Lambda\mid \phi_\lambda=0\}.
\]
Then, in the typed-expansion model class generated by
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}, the following typed data
define a one-state $\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3$ model \(M\): the one-state
\(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the finite intersection site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), the
presheaf of local sections \(F_{p,s}\) constructed below, and the banded groupoid
lift
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
\]
with band \(A_{\mathcal U}\). In that generated model there are a state
\(p\) and a distinguished admissible parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) with
\([r]_p=a\), local slice site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and
\(\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)\), such that the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda.
\]
\item Under the identification in \textup{(i)}, for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$ the corresponding component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ has component obstruction class
\[
\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
\]
satisfying
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
\qquad
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
Consequently,
\[
\mathfrak G_\lambda\ \text{is neutral}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\phi_\lambda=0.
\]
\item $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$. More precisely, if $\Lambda_0=\varnothing$ this is the vacuous terminal-fibre case
\[
\mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing=\mathfrak L_r(a),
\]
while if $\Lambda_0\neq\varnothing$ the terminal objects of $\mathfrak L_r(a)$ lie exactly in the neutral components indexed by $\Lambda_0$ and are in the essential image of $\mathfrak P_r(a)$. In either case
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Lambda_0\neq\varnothing.
\]
\item
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r),
\]
and
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Lambda_0=\varnothing.
\]
\item The aggregate quotients satisfy
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
\]
the universal coefficient sequence reduces to an isomorphism
\[
\eta_A:H^2(N,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A).
\]
For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, let
\[
\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
\]
be the unique class satisfying
\[
\eta_A(\omega_\lambda)=\phi_\lambda,
\]
and choose the normalized representative as follows. If
\(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), set
\[
\alpha_\lambda:=0\in Z^2(N,A).
\]
If \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), choose any normalized simplicial cocycle
\[
\alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)
\]
representing \(\omega_\lambda\), with the alternating ordered-triple
extension of \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}. Thus
\[
\lambda\in\Lambda_0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\omega_\lambda=0,
\]
and, by the normalization just stipulated rather than by an arbitrary choice
of representative, the labels in \(\Lambda_0\) are exactly the labels for
which the construction uses the zero cocycle \(\alpha_\lambda=0\). For
\(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\) the chosen class is nonzero, so any representing
cocycle is nonzero. No assertion is made that every normalized cocycle
representing the zero cohomology class is itself zero.

By \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(i)--(iv), pass to the
finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ appearing in the statement.
The cover $\mathcal U$, its nerve, and the constant-band finite cochain
data are the same, and Leray comparison identifies
$H^2(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})$ with $H^2(N,A)$. The derived component obstruction class
used below is therefore the actual derived class corresponding to the
chosen finite-nerve cocycle.

Define the presheaf of local sections
\[
F_{p,s}:\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}^{op}\to\mathsf{Set}
\]
by
\[
F_{p,s}(U):=\Lambda
\qquad
\text{for every nonempty proper intersection object }U\to a,
\]
\[
F_{p,s}(a):=\Lambda_0,
\]
and
\[
F_{p,s}(\emptyset):=\{\ast\}.
\]
Restrictions between nonempty proper intersection objects are identities,
the restriction maps
\[
F_{p,s}(a)\to F_{p,s}(U)
\]
are the inclusions $\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow \Lambda$, and every
restriction to $\emptyset$ is the unique map.

This is the presheaf \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) of
\Cref{lem:formal-label-sheafification}. That lemma uses the plus--plus
description of sheafification and the strict initial object for empty
pullbacks, so no one-step matching-family description is being assumed.
Since the nonempty nerve is connected, it gives
\[
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda.
\]

For each $\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0$, define a category fibred in groupoids
\[
\mathfrak P_\lambda\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
\]
exactly as in the proof of \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}: on the terminal fibre set
\[
\mathfrak P_\lambda(a):=\varnothing,
\]
and on each nonempty proper formal intersection object $W$, with
\[
I(W):=\{i\in I\mid W\to U_i\},
\]
let the fibre $\mathfrak P_\lambda(W)$ have object set
\[
\{x_i^{(\lambda,W)}\mid i\in I(W)\},
\]
morphism sets equal to \(A_{\mathcal U}(W)\), identified with \(A\) on
connected nonempty \(W\), and composition twisted by the cocycle
\(\alpha_\lambda\) under this identification. Over $\emptyset$ take the
terminal groupoid, with unique restrictions to it. The argument of
\Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction} shows that $\mathfrak P_\lambda$
is a prestack with genuine constant band $A_{\mathcal U}$, that
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_\lambda)(U)\cong\{ \lambda\}
\]
for every nonempty proper intersection object $U\to a$, that
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_\lambda)(a)=\varnothing,
\]
\[
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda)
\qquad
(\lambda\in\Lambda).
\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Exact subgroup classification on torsion-free second homology]\label{cor:torsion-free-h2-classification}
Assume the full hypotheses of \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}: a
fixed finite nerve $N=N(\mathcal U)$ in the finite intersection site associated to a good-cover nerve,
a trivialized constant finite abelian band $A$, the Leray comparison
isomorphism for the finite-nerve classes, the constructed banded groupoid
lift, and essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre. Assume
in addition that
\[
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta
\]
for some integer $\beta\ge 0$. Let $(K_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\Lambda}$ be a family of subgroups of $A$. Then the following are equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item There exist a state $p$, an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ on the fixed nerve $N$, and a bijection
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda
\]
such that the component indexed by $\lambda$ has presentation-relative homological image subgroup
\[
K_\lambda.
\]
\item Every subgroup $K_\lambda$ is generated by at most $\beta$ elements.
\end{enumerate}
When these conditions hold,
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}K_\lambda,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}K_\lambda,
\]
and
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
K_\lambda=0\ \text{for some}\ \lambda\in\Lambda.
\]
In particular, a single component attains surjective homological evaluation
\[
K_\omega=A
\]
if and only if $A$ is generated by at most $\beta$ elements. For $\beta=1$, the attainable single-component presentation-relative homological image subgroups are exactly the cyclic subgroups of $A$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Assume \textup{(i)}. For any component on the fixed finite nerve satisfying the
finite-nerve comparison hypotheses, its presentation-relative homological image subgroup is by
definition the image of the homological evaluation map attached to its component section
obstruction class. Since
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
\]
the universal coefficient comparison identifies that evaluation map with a
homomorphism from $H_2(N,\mathbb Z)$ to $A$. Thus, for each $\lambda$, there
exists a homomorphism
\[
\phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
with
\[
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
After choosing an identification
\[
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta,
\]
the image of $\phi_\lambda$ is generated by the images of the $\beta$ standard basis vectors. Hence $K_\lambda$ is generated by at most $\beta$ elements. Therefore \textup{(ii)} holds.

Assume conversely that \textup{(ii)} holds. For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, choose generators
\[
k_{\lambda,1},\dots,k_{\lambda,\beta}\in K_\lambda
\]
of $K_\lambda$, allowing repetitions and zeros when fewer than $\beta$ generators are needed. Define
\[
\phi_\lambda:\mathbb Z^\beta\to A,
\qquad
(n_1,\dots,n_\beta)\longmapsto \sum_{j=1}^{\beta} n_j k_{\lambda,j}.
\]
Then
\[
\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda)=K_\lambda.
\]
Transporting these maps across the chosen isomorphism
\[
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta
\]
and applying \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction} yields the required state $p$, admissible parameter $r$, and family of component sections. Hence \textup{(i)} holds.

The quotient formulas are those of \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}(v). The global-section criterion follows from \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}(iii), because
\[
K_\lambda=0

 succeeded in 173ms:
presheaf \(F\) and the subset \(\Lambda_0\subseteq\Lambda\), the
sheafification of \(F\) has terminal sections indexed by \(\Lambda\). By
\Cref{prop:stackification-components}, stackification identifies
\[
\pi_0(a(\mathfrak P))\cong aF,
\]
so the components over $a$ are indexed by $\Lambda$.

For a fixed $\lambda$, take on $U_i$ the object $x_i^{(\lambda,U_i)}$ if $\lambda\notin\Lambda_0$ and the distinguished constant object if $\lambda\in\Lambda_0$. On $U_{ij}$ take the morphism $(0,i,j)$ in the nonzero case and the identity in the zero case. The triple defect is
\[
(0,i,k)^{-1}\circ(0,j,k)\circ(0,i,j)
=
(\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,i)
\]
in the twisted case and is $0$ in the constant case. Under the band identification this is exactly $\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}$. Thus the Cech obstruction cocycle of the $\lambda$-component is $\alpha_\lambda$, proving \textup{(v)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Construction with prescribed componentwise homological image subgroups]\label{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}
Let
\[
\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
be a finite good-cover presentation in a pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$ whose nonempty \v{C}ech nerve
\[
N:=N(\mathcal U)
\]
is connected. Equivalently, the geometric realization of the cover nerve is a
paracompact space homotopy equivalent to $|N|$ and all nonempty finite
intersections of members of $\mathcal U$ are acyclic for constant
coefficients. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}; it includes the strict
initial object $\emptyset$ for all empty intersections, so disjoint cover
members have a pullback in the site even though they do not contribute a
nonzero finite-nerve cochain. Let $A$ be a finite abelian group, and let
$A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine constant sheaf on
$\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ whose value on each nonempty basic intersection
is identified with $A$. View $A_{\mathcal U}$ as the automorphism band of
the banded groupoid lift constructed below, and assume
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Use the Leray comparison isomorphism of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv). Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, and let
\[
\phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
be a nonzero homomorphism for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$. Then, in the
typed-expansion model class generated by
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}, the following typed data
define a one-state $\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3$ model \(M\): the one-state
\(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the finite intersection site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), the
presheaf of local sections \(F_{p,s}\) constructed below, and the banded groupoid lift
prestack
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
\]
with band \(A_{\mathcal U}\). In that generated model there are a state
\(p\) and a distinguished admissible parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) with
\([r]_p=a\), local slice site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and
\(\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)\), such that the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda;
\]
\item under the identification in \textup{(i)}, for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$ the corresponding component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ has component obstruction class
\[
\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
\]
satisfying
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
\qquad
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda);
\]
\item $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$ by the vacuous terminal-fibre case
\[
\mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing=\mathfrak L_r(a),
\]
and
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r);
\]
\item the aggregate quotients satisfy
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
\end{enumerate}
In particular, within the fixed finite-nerve and typed-expansion
framework above, every finite family of subgroups of $A$ arising as images of homomorphisms
\[
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
is attained as the family of presentation-relative homological image subgroups of the global component sections of one admissible parameter.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
\]
the universal coefficient sequence reduces to an isomorphism
\[
\eta_A:H^2(N,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A).
\]
For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, choose the unique class
\[
\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
\]
with
\[
\eta_A(\omega_\lambda)=\phi_\lambda,
\]
and choose a normalized simplicial cocycle
\[
\alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)
\]
representing $\omega_\lambda$, with the alternating ordered-triple extension
of \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}.

By \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(i)--(iv), pass to the
finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ appearing in the statement.
The cover $\mathcal U$, its nerve, and the constant-band finite cochain
data are the same, and Leray comparison identifies
$H^2(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})$ with $H^2(N,A)$. The derived component obstruction class
used below is therefore the actual derived class corresponding to the
chosen finite-nerve cocycle.

Define the presheaf of local sections
\[
F_{p,s}:\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}^{op}\to\mathsf{Set}
\]
by
\[
F_{p,s}(U):=\Lambda
\qquad
\text{for every nonempty proper intersection object }U\to a,
\]
\[
F_{p,s}(a):=\varnothing,
\]
and
\[
F_{p,s}(\emptyset):=\{\ast\}.
\]
Restrictions between nonempty proper intersection objects are identities,
and every restriction to $\emptyset$ is the unique map. This is the
presheaf \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) of
\Cref{lem:formal-label-sheafification} with \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\).
That lemma uses the plus--plus description of sheafification, not a
one-step matching-family replacement, and it treats pullbacks equal to
\(\emptyset\) as strict initial objects carrying only the unique section.
Since the nonempty nerve is connected, it gives
\[
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda.
\]

For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, define a category fibred in groupoids
\[
\mathfrak P_\lambda\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
\]
by the nonzero-summand construction of
\Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}. Thus
$\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\varnothing$; over every nonempty proper formal
intersection object $W$ its objects are
$x_i^{(\lambda,W)}$ for $i\in I(W):=\{i\in I\mid W\to U_i\}$; its morphism
sets are the sheaf sections $A_{\mathcal U}(W)$, identified with \(A\) on
connected nonempty \(W\); and composition is twisted by the normalized
cocycle $\alpha_\lambda$ under this identification. Over $\emptyset$ the
fibre is terminal because \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\). The groupoid
laws, strict functoriality, prestack
condition, and genuine constant-band automorphism-sheaf condition are
exactly \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}(i)--(iii), whose banding
uses \Cref{lem:formal-site-constant-sheaf-banding}.

Let
\[
\mathfrak P_r:=\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathfrak P_\lambda.
\]
As in \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}, this is the fibrewise
coproduct over nonempty objects with the shared terminal fibre over
$\emptyset$.
The verification that this is a fibred prestack, that its restrictions are strictly functorial, that its automorphism sheaves are the genuine constant band $A_{\mathcal U}$, and that the normalized cocycle identities give the groupoid laws is precisely \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model} with $\Lambda_0=\varnothing$. On every nonempty proper intersection object $U\to a$, each summand $\mathfrak P_\lambda(U)$ is connected, while there are no morphisms between distinct summands. Consequently,
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(U)\cong \Lambda
\qquad
\text{for every nonempty proper }U\to a,
\]
and
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(a)=\varnothing.
\]
Over $\emptyset$ the shared terminal fibre gives
$\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)(\emptyset)\cong\{\ast\}=F_{p,s}(\emptyset)$.
Hence
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}.
\]
By \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(v)--(vi), the functor
$F_{p,s}$ and the prestack $\mathfrak P_r$ determine a one-state semantic
model $M$ with admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at its unique state $p$,
with base object $[r]_p=a$, local slice site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$,
presheaf of local sections $F_{p,s}$, and banded groupoid lift $\mathfrak P_r$ with
genuine constant band $A_{\mathcal U}$. This admissibility is exactly the expansion convention of
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}; no additional semantic
closure axiom for freely adjoining presheaf or prestack data is being used.
At this point all semantic interfaces required by the statement have been checked: the admissible parameter is $r$; the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve is $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$; the presheaf of local sections is the displayed $F_{p,s}$; the prestack band is the genuine constant sheaf $A_{\mathcal U}$; the cover $\mathcal U$ is compared with derived cohomology by the Leray isomorphism of \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv); the global component sections are the sheafified terminal sections of $F_{p,s}$; and the satisfaction clauses for $\CompSec_s$, $\Sec_s$, and $\NoAmalg_s$ are read from these matching-family and terminal-section data.
By \Cref{prop:stackification-components},
\[
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s},
\]
so the sheafified global sections at $a$ are exactly
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda.
\]
This proves \textup{(i)}.

For each $\lambda\in\Lambda$, let $\mathfrak G_\lambda$ be the full substack of $\mathfrak L_r$ determined by the global component section $\lambda$. On the cover $\mathcal U$, take the lift objects
\[
x_i^{(\lambda)}:=x_i^{(\lambda,U_i)}
\]
and, on each overlap $U_{ij}$, take the distinguished isomorphism
\[
\phi_{ij}^{(\lambda)}:=(0,i,j):x_i^{(\lambda)}|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}x_j^{(\lambda)}|_{U_{ij}}.
\]
Then on every triple overlap $U_{ijk}$,
\[
\bigl(\phi_{ik}^{(\lambda)}\bigr)^{-1}\circ
\phi_{jk}^{(\lambda)}\circ
\phi_{ij}^{(\lambda)}
=
(\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,i),
\]
which identifies with $\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}\in A$ under the automorphism-band identification. Therefore the \v{C}ech cocycle produced by \Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction} is exactly $\alpha_\lambda$, and the component obstruction class of $\mathfrak G_\lambda$ is
\[
\omega_\lambda=[\alpha_\lambda].
\]
This is the stackification-component compatibility clause of \Cref{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model} written on the cover $\mathcal U$.
Thus the finite-nerve comparison uses exactly the fixed good cover
$\mathcal U$, the constant band $A$, and the normalized cocycle
$\alpha_\lambda$ supplied by the homomorphism $\phi_\lambda$; the passage
to derived $H^2(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})$ is the Leray comparison of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv).
Since
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\eta_A(\omega_\lambda)=\phi_\lambda,
\]
one gets
\[
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
This proves \textup{(ii)}.

Because each $\phi_\lambda$ is nonzero and $\eta_A$ is an isomorphism, each class $\omega_\lambda$ is nonzero. By \Cref{prop:derived-branch-classification}, every component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda$ is therefore non-neutral, and \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality} gives
\[
\mathfrak G_\lambda(a)=\varnothing
\qquad
(\forall \lambda\in\Lambda).
\]
Hence
\[
\mathfrak L_r(a)=\varnothing.
\]
Since also
\[
\mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing,
\]
the stackification functor on the terminal fibre is
\[
(\iota_r)_a:\mathfrak P_r(a)\to \mathfrak L_r(a),
\]
that is, the empty functor $\varnothing\to\varnothing$, and it is essentially surjective. Thus $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$.

Moreover,
\[
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\cong \Lambda\neq\varnothing,
\]
and the cover $\mathcal U$ carries an explicit strict matching family:
choose any $\lambda\in\Lambda$ and put the section $\lambda\in
F_{p,s}(U_i)=\Lambda$ on every member $U_i$. Restrictions between
nonempty proper intersections are identities, and restrictions to
$\emptyset$ are unique, so the family agrees on every pullback overlap.
Hence \Cref{def:existence-levels}(ii) gives
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
\]
On the other hand,
\[
F_{p,s}(a)=\varnothing,
\]
so
\[
M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r).
\]
Since $r$ is admissible by construction, it follows that
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
This proves \textup{(iii)}.

Finally, \textup{(iv)} is \Cref{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient,thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient} applied to the family
\[
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda)
\qquad
(\lambda\in\Lambda).
\]
The last sentence is the same statement rewritten in terms of the images of the chosen homomorphisms.
\end{proof}

The preceding construction theorem makes every component gerbe non-neutral. The next theorem removes that restriction and identifies the neutral component gerbes exactly with the zero homomorphisms.

 succeeded in 177ms:
\subsection{Homological images, cokernels, and annihilating characters}

\begin{definition}[Homological image and cokernel]\label{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, define the \emph{presentation-relative homological image subgroup}
\[
K_{\omega}:=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})
\]
and the associated \emph{homological cokernel}
\[
Q_{\omega}:=\operatorname{coker}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})=A/K_{\omega}.
\]
These terms are relative to the presentation-comparison groupoid of
\Cref{def:finite-nerve-comparison-relation}; no presentation-free quotient is
asserted outside that groupoid.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Class-trivializing characters]\label{def:class-admissible-character}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, let
\[
X_{\omega}:=\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi_*(\omega)=0\},
\qquad
\widehat A:=\mathrm{Hom}(A,\mathbb T).
\]
Elements of $X_{\omega}$ are called \emph{class-trivializing} for $\omega$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Character-theoretic detection of the homological cokernel]\label{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:class-admissible-character},
\[
X_{\omega}
=
\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi\circ \mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0\}
=
\mathrm{Ann}(K_{\omega}).
\]
Consequently,
\[
K_{\omega}
=
\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi,
\qquad
Q_{\omega}\cong A\Big/\!\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For every character $\chi:A\to\mathbb T$, naturality of the universal coefficient sequence gives a commutative square
\[
\begin{CD}
H^2(N(\mathcal U),A) @>\eta_A>> \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A) \\
@V\chi_*VV @VV(\chi\circ -)V \\
H^2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb T) @>\eta_{\mathbb T}>> \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),\mathbb T).
\end{CD}
\]
Since $\mathbb T$ is divisible and hence injective in $\mathbf{Ab}$, one has
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),\mathbb T)=0
\]
\cite[Sec.~2.3]{Weibel1994}. Thus $\eta_{\mathbb T}$ is an isomorphism by \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}. Therefore
\[
\chi_*(\omega)=0
\Longleftrightarrow
\eta_{\mathbb T}(\chi_*(\omega))=0
\Longleftrightarrow
\chi\circ \eta_A(\omega)=0
\Longleftrightarrow
\chi\circ \mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0.
\]
This proves the first displayed identity, and the second is simply the definition of the annihilator of a subgroup of $A$.

Let $H:=K_{\omega}=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})\le A$. By the first part of the proof, $X_{\omega}=\mathrm{Ann}(H)$. Finite abelian duality implies
\[
\bigcap_{\chi\in \mathrm{Ann}(H)}\ker\chi=H
\]
\cite[Part~I, Chaps.~2, 12]{Terras1999}. Hence
\[
K_{\omega}
=
\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\]
The final isomorphism follows immediately:
\[
Q_{\omega}
=
A/K_{\omega}
\cong
A\Big/\!\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Character detection of the homological cokernel]\label{cor:intrinsic-character-detection}
The quotient map
\[
\pi_{\omega}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\omega}
\]
induces a natural isomorphism
\[
\widehat{Q_{\omega}}\cong X_{\omega}.
\]
For elements $x,y\in A$, one has
\[
\pi_{\omega}(x)=\pi_{\omega}(y)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\chi(x)=\chi(y)\ \text{for every class-trivializing }\chi.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient},
\[
X_{\omega}=\mathrm{Ann}(K_{\omega})=K_{\omega}^{\perp}.
\]
Characters of $Q_{\omega}=A/K_{\omega}$ are exactly the characters of $A$ that kill $K_{\omega}$, so precomposition with $\pi_{\omega}$ identifies $\widehat{Q_{\omega}}$ with $X_{\omega}$. The separation statement follows from finite abelian duality applied to the finite group $Q_{\omega}$ \cite[Part~I, Chap.~2]{Terras1999}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Initiality among zero-evaluation quotients]\label{thm:intrinsic-pure-ext-initiality}
In the finite nerve representative of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, let
\[
\pi_\omega:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_\omega=A/K_\omega
\]
be the homological cokernel. For every surjective homomorphism $q:A\twoheadrightarrow B$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $q$ factors uniquely through $\pi_\omega$;
\item $q$ kills $\operatorname{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_\omega)=K_\omega$;
\item $\eta_B(q_*\omega)=0$;
\item $q_*(\omega)$ lies in the UCT $\operatorname{Ext}^1$ subgroup
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),B)
\subseteq H^2(N(\mathcal U),B)
\]
of the universal coefficient sequence.
\end{enumerate}
Thus $Q_\omega$ is initial among coefficient quotients for which the pushed-forward obstruction has zero $H_2$ evaluation. This is the pullback-site, finite-nerve form of the companion initiality theorem \cite[Theorem~6.13]{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The equivalence of \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} is the universal property of the quotient
\[
\pi_\omega:A\twoheadrightarrow A/K_\omega.
\]
Indeed, a homomorphism $q:A\to B$ factors uniquely through $\pi_\omega$ if and only if $K_\omega\subseteq\ker q$, which is exactly the assertion that $q$ kills $\operatorname{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_\omega)$.

For \textup{(ii)} and \textup{(iii)}, use naturality of the universal coefficient map with respect to the coefficient homomorphism $q$. The naturality square gives
\[
\eta_B(q_*\omega)=q\circ \eta_A(\omega)=q\circ \mathrm{ev}_\omega.
\]
Thus $\eta_B(q_*\omega)=0$ if and only if $q$ vanishes on the image of $\mathrm{ev}_\omega$, namely on $K_\omega$.

Finally, the universal coefficient exact sequence for the finite nerve is
\[
0\to \operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),B)
\to H^2(N(\mathcal U),B)
\xrightarrow{\eta_B}
\operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),B)
\to 0.
\]
Therefore $\eta_B(q_*\omega)=0$ if and only if $q_*(\omega)$ lies in the kernel of $\eta_B$, which is precisely the displayed UCT $\operatorname{Ext}^1$ subgroup. This proves all equivalences and the initiality statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Class-trivializing initiality when $H_1$ vanishes]\label{cor:h1-vanishing-class-trivializing}
Assume
\[
H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Then, for every surjective homomorphism $q:A\twoheadrightarrow B$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $q_*(\omega)=0$ in $H^2(N(\mathcal U),B)$;
\item $q$ factors uniquely through
\[
\pi_{\omega}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\omega}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Hence, when $H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0$, the homological cokernel is initial among coefficient quotients that annihilate the obstruction class itself.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0$, then
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),B)=0
\]
for every abelian group $B$. Thus, in the criterion of \Cref{thm:intrinsic-pure-ext-initiality}, the zero-evaluation condition is equivalent to $q_*(\omega)=0$, and the conclusion follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Presentation-relative invariance without a canonical splitting]\label{rem:no-canonical-splitting}
For a fixed finite nerve representative, the quotient
$Q_{\omega}$ and the subgroup
$K_{\omega}=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})$ are invariants of the chosen
presentation. Across comparison-equivalent presentations they are transported
functorially only along chosen morphisms in the presentation-comparison
groupoid, as in \Cref{prop:invariance-visible-quotients}; different zigzags
with the same endpoints may carry different coefficient automorphisms. By
contrast, the universal coefficient sequence need not split canonically. Thus
the theory isolates a homological cokernel and homological image subgroup
at each presentation, with comparison-zigzag functoriality, without requiring
any noncanonical decomposition of $\omega$ into $H_2$-evaluated and UCT-Ext
summands.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Comparison with cocycle-coordinate quotients]\label{cor:strict-to-intrinsic-visible}
Let $\alpha$ be any finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative of the class $\omega$. Then the homological cokernel
\[
Q_{\omega}
\]
admits a canonical surjective homomorphism onto the cocycle-coordinate quotient
\[
A/H_\alpha.
\]
Equivalently, every cocycle-coordinate quotient is a quotient of the homological cokernel.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let $q:A\twoheadrightarrow A/H_\alpha$ be the canonical quotient. By \Cref{thm:initial-visible-quotient}, the quotient kills every cocycle value $\alpha_{ijk}$ pointwise, so the pushed-forward cocycle representative is zero. Hence $q_*(\omega)=0$, and in particular it lies in the $\operatorname{Ext}$-subgroup of $H^2(N(\mathcal U),A/H_\alpha)$. Apply \Cref{thm:intrinsic-pure-ext-initiality}.
The equivalence of \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} is immediate from the quotient descriptions
\[
A/H_\alpha
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
Q_{\omega}=A/K_{\omega}.
\]
The equivalence of \textup{(ii)} and \textup{(iii)} is exactly \Cref{prop:chain-vs-homological-visibility}(iii).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Nonzero UCT-Ext classes and vanishing $H_2$ evaluation]\label{thm:character-blind-obstructions}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, with a chosen admissible
parameter, global component section, banded groupoid lift, trivialized constant finite
abelian band $A$, and the \textup{(H4)} comparison datum needed to regard
$\omega$ as a derived component class, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $K_{\omega}=0$;
\item $Q_{\omega}=A$;
\item $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0$;
\item $\omega$ lies in the subgroup
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\subseteq H^2(N(\mathcal U),A).
\]
\end{enumerate}
If these conditions hold and $\omega\neq 0$, then the chosen component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is non-neutral, while every character of $A$ is class-trivializing for that component class. If, in addition, the banded groupoid lift is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre, \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is separated on the slice, and $|\Vis_{p,s}(r)|=1$, then
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient},
\[
K_{\omega}=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}),
\]
so \textup{(i)} and \textup{(iii)} are equivalent. Since $Q_{\omega}=A/K_{\omega}$, conditions \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} are equivalent. By the universal coefficient sequence, \textup{(iii)} holds exactly when $\omega\in\ker(\eta_A)$, and this kernel is the stated $\operatorname{Ext}$-subgroup. Hence all four conditions are equivalent.

If they hold and $\omega\neq 0$, then \Cref{prop:derived-branch-classification} shows that the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is non-neutral.
On the other hand, $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0$ implies
\[
\chi\circ \mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0
\]
for every $\chi\in\widehat A$, so \Cref{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient} shows that every character is class-trivializing.

Assume now that \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is separated on the slice and that $|\Vis_{p,s}(r)|=1$. Since the chosen global component section $v$ exists, \Cref{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches} gives
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
\]
Suppose towards a contradiction that
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r).
\]
By \Cref{def:banded-groupoid-lift}(i), this yields an object of the terminal fibre $(\mathfrak P_r)_a$, and its image under the stackification morphism
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to \mathfrak L_r
\]
defines an object of $\mathfrak L_r(a)$. Because there is only one global component section, every object of $\mathfrak L_r(a)$ lies in the unique component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v(a)$. Hence
\[
\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
By \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}, this implies that $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral, contradicting the non-neutrality already proved. Therefore
\[
M,p\nVdash \Sec_s(r).
\]
Combining this with $M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)$ and admissibility of $r$, we obtain
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Vanishing evaluation when $H_2=0$]\label{cor:h2-vanishing-blindness}
Assume
\[
H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Then, for every obstruction class $\omega\in H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$,
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0,
\qquad
K_{\omega}=0,
\qquad
Q_{\omega}=A.
\]
Every nonzero component obstruction class on such a nerve therefore has zero $H_2$ evaluation, and every character is class-trivializing.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0$, then
\[
\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)=0,
\]
so $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0$ for every $\omega$. Apply \Cref{thm:character-blind-obstructions}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[No nonzero classes with vanishing $H_2$ evaluation when $H_1$ is free]\label{cor:h1-free-no-blindness}
Assume that $H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)$ is free abelian. Then
\[
K_{\omega}=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\omega=0.
\]
Equivalently, every nonzero component obstruction class has nonzero $H_2$ evaluation. In particular, if $|\Vis_{p,s}(r)|=1$, every nonzero component obstruction class on such a nerve produces a proper homological cokernel.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)$ is free abelian, then
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)=0.
\]
Hence the universal coefficient map $\eta_A$ is injective. By \Cref{thm:character-blind-obstructions},
\[
K_{\omega}=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
An \emph{auxiliary register for the homological quotient} for the obstruction class $\omega$ consists of a finite set $R$ and an injective map
\[
\iota_{\omega}:A\hookrightarrow Q_{\omega}\times R
\]
such that
\[
\mathrm{pr}_1\circ\iota_{\omega}=\pi_{\omega}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{corollary}[Fibre size of the homological quotient]\label{cor:intrinsic-hidden-state-lower-bound}
Every auxiliary register for the homological quotient satisfies
\[
|R|\ge |K_{\omega}|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2|R|\ge \log_2|K_{\omega}|.
\]
This lower bound is sharp.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient}, the fibres of $\pi_{\omega}$ are exactly the cosets of $K_{\omega}$, so every fibre has cardinality $|K_{\omega}|$. The same fibre-counting argument as in \Cref{thm:minimal-information-cost-of-strictification} therefore shows that any auxiliary register preserving the first projection must satisfy $|R|\ge |K_{\omega}|$.

For sharpness, take $R:=K_{\omega}$ and choose a set-theoretic section $s_{\omega}:Q_{\omega}\to A$ of $\pi_{\omega}$. Then
\[
\iota_{\omega}(x):=\bigl(\pi_{\omega}(x),x-s_{\omega}(\pi_{\omega}(x))\bigr)
\]
is injective.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Prime decomposition of the homological quotient and fibre size]\label{thm:intrinsic-prime-decomposition}
Write the finite abelian group $A$ as a direct sum of its $p$-primary components:
\[
A=\bigoplus_p A_{(p)}.
\]
Let $\omega_p\in H^2(N(\mathcal U),A_{(p)})$ be the image of $\omega$ under the projection onto the $p$-primary summand. Then
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=\bigoplus_p \mathrm{ev}_{\omega_p},
\qquad
K_{\omega}=\bigoplus_p K_{\omega,p},
\qquad
Q_{\omega}\cong \bigoplus_p A_{(p)}/K_{\omega,p},
\]
where
\[
K_{\omega,p}:=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_p}).
\]
Consequently,
\[
|K_{\omega}|=\prod_p |K_{\omega,p}|,
\qquad
\log_2|K_{\omega}|=\sum_p \log_2|K_{\omega,p}|.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Both $H^2(N(\mathcal U),-)$ and $\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),-)$ commute with finite direct sums, so the universal coefficient map is compatible with the primary decomposition of $A$. Hence
\[
\eta_A(\omega)=\bigoplus_p \eta_{A_{(p)}}(\omega_p),
\]
which is exactly the asserted decomposition of $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}$.

Taking images gives
\[
K_{\omega}
=
\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})
=
\bigoplus_p \mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_p})
=
\bigoplus_p K_{\omega,p},
\]
by \Cref{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient}. The quotient decomposition follows immediately:
\[
Q_{\omega}
=
A/K_{\omega}
\cong
\bigoplus_p A_{(p)}/K_{\omega,p}.
\]
Since finite direct sums correspond to products of cardinalities, the logarithmic fibre-cardinality formula is immediate.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Homological versus chain-coordinate fibre size]\label{cor:strict-intrinsic-budget-comparison}
For every finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative $\alpha$ of the class $\omega$,
\[
|K_{\omega}|\le |H_\alpha|,
\qquad
\log_2|K_{\omega}|\le \log_2|H_\alpha|.
\]
Hence the minimum auxiliary register size for the homological quotient never exceeds that for the cocycle-coordinate quotient. Equality holds if and only if the canonical surjection
\[
Q_{\omega}\twoheadrightarrow A/H_\alpha
\]
is an isomorphism.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{cor:strict-to-intrinsic-visible}, there is a surjective homomorphism
\[
Q_{\omega}=A/K_{\omega}\twoheadrightarrow A/H_\alpha.
\]
For finite groups this implies
\[
|A|/|K_{\omega}|\ge |A|/|H_\alpha|,
\]
hence $|K_{\omega}|\le |H_\alpha|$. The logarithmic inequality is immediate. Equality of cardinalities holds exactly when the displayed surjection is bijective.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Surjective homological evaluation]\label{cor:maximal-collapse}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}:H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)\to A$ is surjective;
\item $K_{\omega}=A$;
\item $Q_{\omega}=0$;
\item the logarithmic fibre cardinality of the homological quotient is $\log_2|A|$:
\[
\log_2|K_{\omega}|=\log_2|A|.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient}, $K_{\omega}=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})$, so \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} are equivalent. Condition \textup{(ii)} is equivalent to \textup{(iii)} by the definition of $Q_{\omega}=A/K_{\omega}$. For finite groups, \textup{(ii)} is also equivalent to $|K_{\omega}|=|A|$, which is exactly \textup{(iv)} after taking $\log_2$.
\end{proof}

538:We study gluing failure in a forcing semantics whose states are sets of realizations. For admitted references, compatible local sectionability is equivalent, on slice-separated reference slices, to nonemptiness of the sheafification of the associated local-object functor. A lower-fragment separation theorem shows that compatible local sections, global sections, and gluing-level nullity are undefinable in the automorphism-invariant, constant-free, one-variable fragment of the lower reduct. At the groupoid-valued level, representative rigidity and global conservativity force surjectivity of the sheafification unit at the terminal fibre; with $H^1$-vanishing this is equivalent to the local object functor already being a sheaf there. Hence gluing-level nullity cannot be recovered from a strict functorial choice of local representatives. For banded realization-stack extensions, each visible branch determines a canonical Giraud class in $H^2$. For finite abelian bands this yields a homological evaluation map whose image controls a presentation-object visible-quotient invariant, while the character-blind case is exactly the pure $\operatorname{Ext}$ contribution. On fixed finite nerves with $H_1=0$ and $H_2\cong\mathbb Z^\beta$, mixed realizations produce precisely the hidden subgroups generated by at most $\beta$ elements; on bouquet good covers, a nonzero finite abelian group occurs as a pure two-branch resolution kernel exactly when $d(G)\le 2\beta$ and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group.
572:The authors used AI-assisted tools for editorial review, language polishing,
584:Sheaf-theoretic semantics offers a natural language for the passage from local data to global objects. In logical applications, and in particular in sheaf-theoretic analyses of contextuality \cite{AbramskyBrandenburger2011,AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012,AbramskyBarbosaKishidaLalMansfield2015,Caru2017,Caru2018,Montanhano2021}, the central question is whether compatible local families glue to global sections. The classical tools for measuring the failure of gluing are gerbes, obstruction classes, and cohomological invariants \cite{Giraud1971,Breen2006,Hatcher2002}. In this paper we study that question in a forcing semantics whose states are sets of realizations, with the aim of separating the successive layers at which genuinely new local-to-global structure appears.
591:The semantics is thus related both to team and imperfect-information semantics \cite{Hodges1997,Vaananen2007,AbramskyPuljujarviVaananen2025} and to the forcing and sheaf-theoretic tradition stemming from Beth, Kripke, Fitting, and Tierney and later developed in topos-theoretic form \cite{Beth1956,Kripke1965,Fitting1969,Tierney1972,Goldblatt2006,Johnstone2002}.
595:The second part begins once the set-valued local-object functor is enriched by groupoid-valued data. \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity} gives a rigidity statement for representative-rigid lifts: if the local-object presheaf is slice-separated and a realization prestack admits a strict functorial choice of representatives and is globally conservative at the terminal fibre, then the sheafification unit is surjective there; under $H^1$-vanishing and the same slice-separatedness, this is equivalent to the local object functor already being a sheaf at the terminal fibre. Thus gluing-level nullity is necessarily tied to genuinely gluing-sensitive lift data. \Cref{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension} identifies the canonicity that survives at this level: once the sheafified local object functor has been lifted to a banded realization stack, the branch obstruction map to derived $H^2$ is intrinsic up to equivalence of banded extensions. For such an extension, visible value classes are the global sections of the sheaf of connected components (\Cref{thm:visible-value-components}), each visible branch determines a banded component gerbe (\Cref{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition}), and, on slice-separated reference slices, \Cref{thm:gerbe-null-semantics} identifies gluing-level absence with the existence of visible branches together with non-neutrality of the corresponding component gerbes. No general existence theorem for gluing-sensitive lifts is proved; Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix-ghz} gives an explicit GHZ/Mermin example of chosen lift data.
615:At the passage from set-valued to groupoid-valued data, \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity} shows that a slice-separated representative-rigid lift can be globally conservative only when the sheafification unit at the terminal fibre is surjective; under $H^1$-vanishing and slice-separatedness on the reference slice, this is equivalent to sheafiness of the local object functor at that fibre. Thus the gerbe-theoretic part of the theory is necessarily relative to additional lift data. \Cref{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension} identifies the maximal canonicity available without an existence theorem: once the sheafified local object functor has been lifted to a banded realization stack, the branch obstruction class is intrinsic up to equivalence of such extensions. Visible value classes are then governed by the sheaf of connected components; on slice-separated reference slices, strict gluing failure is carried by the component gerbes of the visible branches.

5481:The conceptual rigidity theorem is also mostly a formal consequence of stackification: terminal essential surjectivity forces surjectivity on component sheaves, while \(H^1=0\) kills the Isom-torsor for the converse. Its only delicate step is realizing every global component section by a global stack object, and the manuscript obtains that by imposing a very strong “representative-rigid” splitting plus slice-separatedness.

5915:The literature search confirms that the surrounding program is established: Abramsky–Mansfield–Barbosa already define Čech cohomology obstructions to global sections, Carù studies their detection limits and torsor interpretation, and later work develops complete/semi-module and bundle formulations. I’m checking whether the manuscript’s gerbe and quotient constructions add an invariant with independent content, or only apply standard \(H^2\), UCT, annihilator, and subgroup formulas to newly named semantic data.

D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\a9\bridge_out.txt:861:.\main.tex:70:We study gluing failure in a forcing semantics whose states are sets of realizations. For admitted references, compatible local sectionability is equivalent, on slice-separated reference slices, to nonemptiness of the sheafification of the associated local-object functor. A lower-fragment separation theorem shows that compatible local sections, global sections, and gluing-level nullity are undefinable in the automorphism-invariant, constant-free, one-variable fragment of the lower reduct. At the groupoid-valued level, representative rigidity and global conservativity force surjectivity of the sheafification unit at the terminal fibre; with $H^1$-vanishing this is equivalent to the local object functor already being a sheaf there. Hence gluing-level nullity cannot be recovered from a strict functorial choice of local representatives. For banded realization-stack extensions, each visible branch determines a canonical Giraud class in $H^2$. For finite abelian bands this yields a homological evaluation map whose image controls a presentation-object visible-quotient invariant, while the character-blind case is exactly the pure $\operatorname{Ext}$ contribution. D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\a9\bridge_out.txt:861:.\main.tex:70:We study gluing failure in a forcing semantics whose states are sets of realizations. For admitted references, compatible local sectionability is equivalent, on slice-separated reference slices, to nonemptiness of the sheafification of the associated local-object functor. A lower-fragment separation theorem shows that compatible local sections, global sections, and gluing-level nullity are undefinable in the automorphism-invariant, constant-free, one-variable fragment of the lower reduct. At the groupoid-valued level, representative rigidity and global conservativity force surjectivity of the sheafification unit at the terminal fibre; with $H^1$-vanishing this is equivalent to the local object functor already being a sheaf there. Hence gluing-level nullity cannot be recovered from a strict functorial choice of local representatives. For banded realization-stack extensions, each visible branch determines a canonical Giraud class in $H^2$. For finite abelian bands this yields a homological evaluation map whose image controls a presentation-object visible-quotient invariant, while the character-blind case is exactly the pure $\operatorname{Ext}$ contribution. On fixed finite nerves with $H_1=0$ and $H_2\cong\mathbb Z^\beta$, mixed realizations produce precisely the hidden subgroups generated by at most $\beta$ elements; on bouquet good covers, a nonzero finite abelian group occurs as a pure two-branch resolution kernel exactly when $d(G)\le 2\beta$ and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group.

An independent assessment placed this manuscript at TIER-4 and judged its content REPACKAGING, because Abramsky-Mansfield-Barbosa, "The Cohomology of Non-Locality and Contextuality" (2012), already encodes failure of global sections by Cech classes. The decisive criticism was that the manuscript is "too conditional and synthetically engineered": its strongest results, including the characterisation that a nonzero finite abelian G occurs as a pure two-branch resolution kernel exactly when d(G) <= 2*beta and G is not a cyclic p-group, hold on a model class that was defined in order to admit them. A referee reads that as design, not discovery.

The assessment named one lever that would change this:

  Prove that every finite Abramsky-Brandenburger empirical model E canonically, and functorially for refinements, determines a banded realization stack of the kind this paper studies.

That is the task. It matters because it converts theorems about a bespoke class into theorems about the standard objects of contextuality theory. If it holds, the paper's machinery speaks about every finite empirical model, and the synthetic-class objection disappears.

Do this:

1. Read Abramsky-Brandenburger's sheaf-theoretic framework and Abramsky-Mansfield-Barbosa carefully enough to state the correspondence precisely. Search the literature (arXiv API, Crossref, Semantic Scholar, zbMATH) for anyone who has already built this bridge â€” if it exists, say so and stop.

2. State the correspondence as a theorem with explicit hypotheses: which empirical models, which site, which band, and in what sense the assignment is functorial under refinement of measurement contexts. Be precise about what "canonically" means here.

3. Prove it, or find exactly where it fails. A precisely located failure is a real result: if some class of empirical models cannot be realized this way, that obstruction is itself worth stating.

4. If it holds, work out what the paper's existing theorems then say about empirical models â€” in particular whether the d(G) <= 2*beta characterisation transfers, and whether the Giraud H^2 class recovers or refines the AMB cohomological obstruction. Do not overclaim a refinement you have not proved.

Also fix the weakest link the assessment named: lem:generated-subsite-model-interface clause (iv) â€” contractibility in the ambient good-cover realization does not by itself establish the asserted degeneration. Either prove it or weaken the statement to what holds.

  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:582:\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:583:
> D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:584:Sheaf-theoretic semantics offers a natural language 
for the passage from local data to global objects. In logical applications, and in particular in sheaf-theoretic 
analyses of contextuality \cite{AbramskyBrandenburger2011,AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012,AbramskyBarbosaKishidaLalM
ansfield2015,Caru2017,Caru2018,Montanhano2021}, the central question is whether compatible local families glue to 
global sections. The classical tools for measuring the failure of gluing are gerbes, obstruction classes, and 
cohomological invariants \cite{Giraud1971,Breen2006,Hatcher2002}. In this paper we study that question in a forcing 
semantics whose states are sets of realizations, with the aim of separating the successive layers at which genuinely 
new local-to-global structure appears.
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:585:
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:586:Our starting point is the extension chain
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:587:\[
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:588:\mathbb{L}_0 \xhookrightarrow{\iota} \mathbb{L}_1 
\preceq \mathbb{L}_2 \preceq \mathbb{L}_3 \preceq \mathbb{L}_4,
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:589:\]
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:590:where $\mathbb{L}_0$ is the pointwise layer, 
$\mathbb{L}_1$ is the information-state forcing layer, $\mathbb{L}_2$ adds local objects and site structure, 
$\mathbb{L}_3$ adds branchwise obstruction data, and $\mathbb{L}_4$ adds refinement dynamics. The map 
$\mathbb{L}_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}_1$ is a singleton semantic embedding, whereas the later steps are conservative 
extensions in the sense of \Cref{def:conservative-extension}. Thus the geometric and homological material introduced 
later does not alter the forcing behaviour of formulas already present in the lower layers.
> D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:591:The semantics is thus related both to team and 
imperfect-information semantics \cite{Hodges1997,Vaananen2007,AbramskyPuljujarviVaananen2025} and to the forcing and 
sheaf-theoretic tradition stemming from Beth, Kripke, Fitting, and Tierney and later developed in topos-theoretic form 
\cite{Beth1956,Kripke1965,Fitting1969,Tierney1972,Goldblatt2006,Johnstone2002}.
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:592:
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:593:The first part of the paper concerns the passage 
from information states to local objects. \Cref{prop:compsec-to-sheafification} shows that compatible local 
sectionability gives nonemptiness of the associated sheafification, and \Cref{thm:sheafification-characterization} 
proves the converse on slice-separated reference slices. We then prove in \Cref{thm:pointwise-irreducibility} that the 
predicates $\CompSec_s$, $\Sec_s$, and $\Null^{\mathrm{glue}}_s$ are not definable by automorphism-invariant, 
constant-free formulas of the $\mathbb{L}_1$-reduct with one free reference variable. This is only a lower-bound 
statement for the comparison fragment under consideration, but it isolates a genuine change of behaviour between 
$\mathbb L_1$ and $\mathbb L_2$.
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:594:
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:595:The second part begins once the set-valued 
local-object functor is enriched by groupoid-valued data. \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity} gives a rigidity 
statement for representative-rigid lifts: if the local-object presheaf is slice-separated and a realization prestack 
admits a strict functorial choice of representatives and is globally conservative at the terminal fibre, then the 
sheafification unit is surjective there; under $H^1$-vanishing and the same slice-separatedness, this is equivalent to 
the local object functor already being a sheaf at the terminal fibre. Thus gluing-level nullity is necessarily tied to 
genuinely gluing-sensitive lift data. \Cref{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension} identifies the canonicity that 
survives at this level: once the sheafified local object functor has been lifted to a banded realization stack, the 
branch obstruction map to derived $H^2$ is intrinsic up to equivalence of banded extensions. For such an extension, 
visible value classes are the global sections of the sheaf of connected components 
(\Cref{thm:visible-value-components}), each visible branch determines a banded component gerbe 
(\Cref{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition}), and, on slice-separated reference slices, \Cref{thm:gerbe-null-semantics} 
identifies gluing-level absence with the existence of visible branches together with non-neutrality of the 
corresponding component gerbes. No general existence theorem for gluing-sensitive lifts is proved; 
Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix-ghz} gives an explicit GHZ/Mermin example of chosen lift data.

> D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:9352:2. **IS IT ACTUALLY NEW? REPACKAGING.** 
Abramsky–Mansfield–Barbosa, *The Cohomology of Non-Locality and Contextuality* (2012), already encode failure of 
global sections by Čech classes; Giraud’s *Cohomologie non abélienne* classifies banded gerbes by \(H^2\), while 
Okay–Raussendorf develop the corresponding homotopical contextuality framework. The displayed classification results 
from freely installing prescribed maps \(\mathbb Z^\beta\to G\) and applying the standard structure theorem for finite 
abelian groups, so the terminology is new but the mathematical content is not.
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:9353:
  D:\omega\automath\tools\chatgpt-oracle\sprint\assess\A9.txt:9354:3. **DEPTH OF THE MATHEMATICS.** Mostly 
bookkeeping: stackification, Giraud \(H^2\), the universal coefficient theorem, character annihilators, quotient 
identities, and finite-abelian decomposition are assembled carefully, but no new obstruction mechanism or hard 
argument is introduced.

+请对本稿进行“研究机会审计”，而非核验、复述或延伸现有证明：依据下述已证结果与工具，判断本邻域内真正可达的最强定理，并优先寻找可由这些工具攻击的文献中具名开放问题。
+背景：本稿曾被 Annals of Pure and Applied Logic 拒稿，但编辑未评价数学内容，仅认为术语非标准且未解释；现已全面改用标准术语，因此请将自己视为首位实质审读其数学内容的外部专家。
+另有独立评估将其判为 TIER-4、内容为 REPACKAGING：Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa（2012）已用 Čech 类编码全局截面失败，而本稿最强结果成立于为容纳该结果而定义的模型类，故被认为是设计而非发现。
+诚实定位：当前机器只作用于作者给定的站点、带及非分裂带状群胚提升；尚未得到关于该领域既有标准对象的新定理。
+
+【已证结果（标准术语）】
+1. 对切片上分离的集合值预层 (F)，给定带为交换群层 \(\mathscr A\) 的群胚纤维化、其栈化及 \(\pi_0\cong aF\)，每个全局连通分量截面确定一个 \(\mathscr A\)-带状 gerbe；在终端纤维本质满射及 Čech--导出比较假设下，无整体粘合的匹配族等价于存在全局分量截面且所有相应 gerbe 均非中性，其证书为导出 Giraud 类 \(\omega_v\in H^2\)。
+2. 对有限 nerve、常有限交换带 (A)，UCT 给出 \(\operatorname{ev}_\omega:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A\)；其像 (K_\omega) 与余核 (A/K_\omega) 分别由湮灭特征完全恢复，且 (A/K_\omega) 是使推出类仅余纯 \(\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1,-)\) 部分的初始系数商；纯 Ext 类恰是 (H_2)-评价与全部特征均不可见的非零类。
+3. 对有限多个分量，保留分量标签与统一特征所见的两个商分别为 (A/\bigcap_vK_v) 与 (A/\sum_vK_v)，并有核为 \((\sum_vK_v)/(\bigcap_vK_v)\) 的短正合列。
+4. 在连通有限好覆盖交叠站点、(H_1(N)=0) 时，可由显式 (2)-余循环带状预栈同时实现任意有限族同态 (H_2(N)\to A)，精确控制中性/非中性分量、像子群及上述聚合商。
+5. 若 (H_2(N)\cong\mathbb Z^\beta)，可实现的像子群恰为至多由 \(\beta\) 个元素生成者；对选定的 \(\bigvee_1^\beta S^2\) 开星好覆盖，非零有限交换群 (G) 成为两个互补非零像子群的标签遗忘核，当且仅当 (d(G)\le2\beta) 且 (G) 非循环 (p)-群。此为所选数据的存在分类，不是既有对象的内在不变量。
+
+【现有工具箱】
+带状群胚提升与栈化；全局分量的 gerbe 分解；无效下降数据的 Čech (2)-余循环证书及 Leray/Čech--导出比较；Giraud (H^2) 的中性判据与带变换自然性；UCT 评价、纯 Ext 分离、有限交换群特征对偶；坐标像与同调像的比较；按指定 (H_2\to A) 构造预栈及混合中性分量；交/和聚合与正合列；精确辅助寄存器界 \(|H_\alpha|,|K_\omega|,(|\sum K_v|/|\cap K_v|),\max_v|K_v|\)；开星好覆盖、有限 nerve 与细分比较；代表严格且拉回稳定的提升在分离性与 (H^1=0) 下强迫终端处已满足层条件的刚性定理；低阶单变量无常元片段的不可定义性证书。
+
+【已尝试且失败，勿再次建议】
+1. “每个有限 Abramsky--Brandenburger 经验模型规范且对语境细化函子地给出所需非分裂带状实现栈”失败：规范构造只有 \(\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e)\times B\mathscr A\)，其全部分量 gerbe 中性、(H^2) 类及同调像皆为零。
+2. AMB 障碍是由局部截面索引的相对 Čech (H^1) 扭子类；本稿是由全局分量截面索引、层带系数的导出 (H^2) gerbe 类。该次数及索引域失配使强语境模型上的本稿映射定义域为空，故不存在覆盖所有有限经验模型的恢复或细化。
+3. 支持层对应虽函子，但在 AB 框架中兼容支持族与全局支持赋值本就是同一集合，故不能产生新的“匹配但不粘合”结论；(d(G)\le2\beta) 分类也不转移至裸经验模型，因为其不选择 nerve、带或非分裂 (2)-余循环数据。
+4. GHZ/Mermin 的符号线提升仍是分裂提升，不能恢复 AMB 的非零 (H^1) 类；严格拉回稳定的代表选择也不能承载该粘合失败，因为刚性定理迫使终端层化单位满射。
+5. 现有同调商仅沿指定展示比较曲折函子地传输，不具路径无关的展示自由性；(H_2)-评价对纯 Ext 类盲，因此不得把它建议为完整不变量。
+
+【可直接引用而无需重证】
+可直接调用：Giraud/Stacks Project 的带状 gerbe--(H^2) 分类与中性判据；Mac Lane--Moerdijk 的层化/下降；好覆盖的 nerve/Leray 比较；同调上同调 UCT 与有限交换群结构、Ext、特征对偶；Abramsky--Brandenburger（2011）的经验模型支持层框架；Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa（2012）的相对 Čech (H^1) 障碍；Carù（2017）的扭子解释；Barbosa--Kharoof--Okay 的测量情景、丛及单纯丛函子。
+
+【请回答的核心问题】
+在上述 (H^1/H^2) 次数失配已关闭显然桥梁后，是否仍有任何路线使该机器作用于学界既有对象，尤其是高阶 gerbe、(2)-扭子、栈的障碍理论，或其他以层带导出 (H^2) 为自然不变量的标准场景？
+请提出至多三项候选，并逐项给出：(a) 可望证明的精确定理陈述；(b) 若为具名开放问题，问题名称、提出者、准确出处（论文/专著、节或问题编号/页码）及其截至目前仍开放的依据；(c) 现有机器可直接承担的步骤；(d) 尚缺的结构、引理、估计或规范性，以及最可能失败的反例机制；(e) 主观成功概率、成功后的期刊层级影响，并按“成功概率×层级影响”排序。
+请明确区分“一篇后续论文可补齐”与“需要新的研究纲领”。若不存在具名开放问题，请给出最强的非具名但外部自然定理；若连此亦不可达，请直言“该机器达不到该层级”并说明结构性原因，这同样是有价值的结论。
+最后，请在不以改名或重写包装冒充数学提升的前提下，给出本稿当前的诚实学术上限、最合适的投稿层级及具体期刊候选。
+不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

+请对本稿进行“研究机会审计”，而非核验、复述或延伸现有证明：依据下述已证结果与工具，判断本邻域内真正可达的最强定理，并优先寻找可由这些工具攻击的文献中具名开放问题。
+背景：本稿曾被 Annals of Pure and Applied Logic 拒稿，但编辑未评价数学内容，仅认为术语非标准且未解释；现已全面改用标准术语，因此请将自己视为首位实质审读其数学内容的外部专家。
+另有独立评估将其判为 TIER-4、内容为 REPACKAGING：Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa（2012）已用 Čech 类编码全局截面失败，而本稿最强结果成立于为容纳该结果而定义的模型类，故被认为是设计而非发现。
+诚实定位：当前机器只作用于作者给定的站点、带及非分裂带状群胚提升；尚未得到关于该领域既有标准对象的新定理。
+
+【已证结果（标准术语）】
+1. 对切片上分离的集合值预层 (F)，给定带为交换群层 \(\mathscr A\) 的群胚纤维化、其栈化及 \(\pi_0\cong aF\)，每个全局连通分量截面确定一个 \(\mathscr A\)-带状 gerbe；在终端纤维本质满射及 Čech--导出比较假设下，无整体粘合的匹配族等价于存在全局分量截面且所有相应 gerbe 均非中性，其证书为导出 Giraud 类 \(\omega_v\in H^2\)。
+2. 对有限 nerve、常有限交换带 (A)，UCT 给出 \(\operatorname{ev}_\omega:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A\)；其像 (K_\omega) 与余核 (A/K_\omega) 分别由湮灭特征完全恢复，且 (A/K_\omega) 是使推出类仅余纯 \(\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1,-)\) 部分的初始系数商；纯 Ext 类恰是 (H_2)-评价与全部特征均不可见的非零类。
+3. 对有限多个分量，保留分量标签与统一特征所见的两个商分别为 (A/\bigcap_vK_v) 与 (A/\sum_vK_v)，并有核为 \((\sum_vK_v)/(\bigcap_vK_v)\) 的短正合列。
+4. 在连通有限好覆盖交叠站点、(H_1(N)=0) 时，可由显式 (2)-余循环带状预栈同时实现任意有限族同态 (H_2(N)\to A)，精确控制中性/非中性分量、像子群及上述聚合商。
+5. 若 (H_2(N)\cong\mathbb Z^\beta)，可实现的像子群恰为至多由 \(\beta\) 个元素生成者；对选定的 \(\bigvee_1^\beta S^2\) 开星好覆盖，非零有限交换群 (G) 成为两个互补非零像子群的标签遗忘核，当且仅当 (d(G)\le2\beta) 且 (G) 非循环 (p)-群。此为所选数据的存在分类，不是既有对象的内在不变量。
+
+【现有工具箱】
+带状群胚提升与栈化；全局分量的 gerbe 分解；无效下降数据的 Čech (2)-余循环证书及 Leray/Čech--导出比较；Giraud (H^2) 的中性判据与带变换自然性；UCT 评价、纯 Ext 分离、有限交换群特征对偶；坐标像与同调像的比较；按指定 (H_2\to A) 构造预栈及混合中性分量；交/和聚合与正合列；精确辅助寄存器界 \(|H_\alpha|,|K_\omega|,(|\sum K_v|/|\cap K_v|),\max_v|K_v|\)；开星好覆盖、有限 nerve 与细分比较；代表严格且拉回稳定的提升在分离性与 (H^1=0) 下强迫终端处已满足层条件的刚性定理；低阶单变量无常元片段的不可定义性证书。
+

---
name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence â‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter â‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion â‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
âœ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
âŒ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
âœ… Write â†’ Run (pass) â†’ Revert fix â†’ Run (MUST FAIL) â†’ Restore â†’ Run (pass)
âŒ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
âœ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
âŒ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
âœ… Re-read plan â†’ Create checklist â†’ Verify each â†’ Report gaps or completion
âŒ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
âœ… Agent reports success â†’ Check VCS diff â†’ Verify changes â†’ Report actual state
âŒ Trust agent report
```

## Why This Matters

From 24 failure memories:
- your human partner said "I don't believe you" - trust broken
- Undefined functions shipped - would crash
- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features
- Time wasted on false completion â†’ redirect â†’ rework
- Violates: "Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced."

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

## The Bottom Line

**No shortcuts for verification.**

Run the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.

This is non-negotiable.
